llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7742:| **High.** The argument fails for authorization established *inside* the mutation: a viewer who may write a hidden gate row enables it, binds the expiring page of that gate's room, disables it; abort-versus-commit then shows the unswept population of a prefix no query by that viewer could examine | GPT 1 | Accepted, conservatively: a new condition, *a stable verdict* — no write to a table the page's read rule depends on (each table its `exists` probes name, and a probed maintain's source) can execute before the page's scan: none earlier in statement order, none inside a loop that encloses the scan. The broader contract — transaction-local authorization as an accepted disclosure — is **not** proposed; it would be the author's ruling |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7745:| Missing fixtures: the same addressed prefix under a denied verdict; the gate program; traversal through bound values, helpers, predicates, condition setup, nesting, shapes and serialized IR; sweep then a fresh retry with the failed receipt unchanged | GPT 3 | Accepted |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7760:| **High.** A stable verdict is not a stable examined population. With `read <- .owner = viewer`, a blind update the viewer's write authority admits can set a hidden row's `owner` to the viewer and its expiry to the past; a page over the viewer's own prefix `[viewer, 'A']` then examines that row or does not, and `E_BOUND` versus the program's own refusal reveals the hidden row's retained column. D(page) is empty — the rule has no probe — and a snapshot query of the prefix is empty in both worlds | GPT 1 | The page's *table*, and every maintain source that can change it, must also be unwritten before the scan |

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7977:### Decisions and dispositions
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7983:| A synced read-through upsert summarized as an online-only read | QX (2026-09-30) supersedes the blanket queueing restriction; round 4's generated-rewrite withdrawal stands | Eligible read-through upserts use §20.5's acquired-coverage proof. The obsolete server-only note and its branch-pattern pointer are removed. Honest insert limitations (horizon, unpinned key, unique/cap coverage) remain. The card keeps explicit branching only for still-blocked `insert … if absent`; no automatic `--fix` is offered. |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7984:| `len(text)` / `length(text)` | Fixed by AG, accepted aliases | Lower to `count` before planning, with identical text-character/list semantics, type checks and meters. An explicitly declared helper keeps its meaning. This deliberately narrows §5.5's one-spelling ruling: an exact, familiar synonym costs no backend capability and the repeated cold-builder loop is avoidable. |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7987:| Notification helper falls through without `return` | Fixed by AG, accepted | Block helpers fall through to null, like a bare `return`; a final expression is not implicitly returned. Explicit returns retain their values, including returns from nested branches. |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7989:| Complete digest helper also called by unused viewer preview | Deliberately refused; MP's contextual reason retained, rewrite fixed by AG | No helper grants system authority and a first-50 truncation is not the same total. The printed replacement is a comment for the entire unused query declaration; its caret and explicit line range identify that declaration. The failed helper read and caller chain remain in the reason; the job and complete helper remain. Removal is an author choice, not `--fix`. |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7990:| Nonunique group-title lookup emits `N_NEVER_COMPLETE` | Deliberately retains its server completeness class; advice fixed by AG | A title does not imply a unique key. For a synced, id-authorized lookup, the note prints the complete original query with `held` added as a manual alternative. Predicate, ordering, duplicate matches and shape stay identical; device-only execution requires acquired coverage and has no server fallback. No uniqueness constraint or invented id parameter is introduced. |

llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:5:**Systems:** Snapback 4 — Quarry column declarations (`on delete`), the binder (eligibility, bounds, index synthesis, `check` notes), the schema IR and its capability fence, the engine's delete path on the server SQLite store (and the evaluation Postgres store through the same runner), commit evidence (`/changes` tables and footprints), both device stores and prediction, partition events, activation, the Lean model and its oracle, the difftest generators, the simulator, the guide cards and the wire refusal codes
llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:8:**Revised:** 2026-10-12 (Integration 3: retained event allowance counts every union arm); 2026-10-12 (revision 8: revision-7 GPT findings — export event prices/envelopes before private derivations are projected away, price receiver transition steps, and accept only stable name/literal proof keys; Muse cascade-through paired regression added); 2026-10-12 (revision 7: revision-6 GPT findings — schema-fixed pending-event heap retained for the request, audience-transition tables included in the publication envelope, stable bindings in every child-inserting status-proof program; Muse lifetime-seek term fixed); 2026-10-12 (revision 6: fresh revision-5 GPT findings — action hooks run within the prepaid physical scope, lifetime-hook heap overlaps are reserved, derived private-field delivery targets are refused, and status aliases require stable bindings); 2026-10-12 (revision 5: round-5 GPT fixes — gather set-null groups before writing, bypass child read rules in constraints, sum concurrent write heap reservations, queue retained device history, and prove count guards at each delete site; reconciles development reset and the retired orphan note); 2026-10-12 (renumbered from 3000.038, which file assets took when 0.4.23 merged; review files renamed to match); 2026-10-12 (revision 4: folds the third delta round, a review of revision 3 with its implementation: GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. Every walk streams pages through the reference index and keeps identities only; a restrict probe streams one row per seek past the closure, the root included, to its first outside witness; set null rereads each row before clearing it; the reply's event count is decided by the static action envelope; the reservation prices the pages and charges output. Field-owned references carry no action, stated. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 3: folds the second delta round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. §5.1 is restated meter by meter, as the code computes it, with the per-probe witness, two seeks for every expiring discovery and identity-only retention. The reference index seeks a table's effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`). Every removal, the root included, uses the row's current image. Row-keyed view member tables are refused as cascade and set-null targets. The §4.4 guard is noted as incompatible with `set null`. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 2: folds the delta round, GPT and Muse both NOT READY. The reservation is defined from schema-fixed closure bounds: closure identities, closure-skipping probes, read-through seeks and a physical allowance, with heap as a maximum. The published touched set includes every derived destination, and the reply omits every row the actions changed. Cascade and set null are refused into tables that feed a maintained sum, have any field-owned column, or belong to a timeline in any role. `set null` is refused where any rule hops through the column. A device needs the referencing group acquired, not only readable. The removal order follows cascade and `read through` edges, whose union must be acyclic. `check` names the read prerequisite where a deleter may not be a reader (`N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ`). Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (revision 1: folds one blind round, GPT NOT READY and Muse READY WITH CHANGES. `on delete keep` is added for deliberate history. A delete evaluates its whole cascade closure before it refuses, so a diamond or a sibling reference never refuses spuriously, and the reported reference is chosen in schema order, never by hidden iteration order. `set null` may clear only a column that carries no authority anywhere in the schema. Every published trace of a delete with actions — touched tables, change footprints, event counts, the physical authorization allowance — is fixed by the schema. The reservation reuses the exported source-write price, so a device consumes the same price the server computes. A null-exempt cap never proves a population bound. Cascade and set null into append-only, field-owned or unpriced children are refused. The postcondition is scoped to the rows present when the delete runs. Dispositions in §11); 2026-10-12 (draft)

llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:136:| `cascade` | `cap N by <column>` on the child (exactly that column), or a plain single-column `unique <column>` key (an inferred effective bound of one) | without either bound: `E_CASCADE_BOUND`. A composite unique key does not bound one reference group. With `E_REFERENCE_ACTION`: a cycle in the cascade graph, including a self-reference, or in the union of cascade and `read through` edges (§3 step 6); a child that is `append only`, has any field-owned (`written by`) column, has a derived synced destination that delivers private fields to their owners, feeds a maintained `sum` (view or column), plays any role in a timeline (owned, mapped or authors), is a row-keyed view's member table (its writes may recount a group outside any fixed price), or whose source writes have no fixed price (a `server_only` reservation plan, LLP 3000.022 §14.3); a `read through` reference whose child can carry a finite effective deadline (§6.1). |
llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:137:| `set null` | an optional (`?`) reference; either `cap N by <column>` or the same single-column unique inference, whose effective bound is one | everything refused for `cascade` except the cycle rule (set null does not recurse), and a column that **carries authority or meaning anywhere in the schema**: `immutable`, `written by`, a `sequence` parent, the root of a recorded or maintained path (`.column.field`), read by **any** rule of **any** table — the child's own read and write rules (directly or as the first hop of `.column.field`), every other table's `exists`/`created` probe that keys or equates on it, and every rule's hop that passes through it (`.task.column`) — named by the child's `sync` audience or horizon index, or named by any `cap` other than `cap N by <column>` alone. (Another table's sync audience can depend on the column only through a `read through` capture of the child's read rule, which the read-rule exclusion already covers.) |

llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:671:## 7. `check`, diagnostics and the wire
llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:677:| `E_CASCADE_BOUND` | `check` error | a `cascade`/`set null` without an explicit `cap N by <column>` or an inferred cap of one from plain single-column `unique <column>`, or a delete whose reservation cannot fit its program's ceilings (§5.2) |
llp/3000.039-a-reference-declares-what-deleting-its-row-does.rfc.md:680:| `N_DELETE_RESTRICTED` | `check` note, review tier | a mutation or job deletes a row of `P`, and an **undeclared**, non-`read through` reference into `P` or into any table `P`'s cascades reach (now `restrict`) can refuse it; names each such `child.column`, says this used to keep the children, and gives the four declarations. Declaring any action on the column silences it, as does a guard that proves no such row exists at the delete — the 0.4 orphans note's guards, carried over (LLP 3000.036 §16.2, §17.4): a top-level `require <count>[<the deleted key>] = 0` earlier than the delete, with no write of the child between (rebinding, helper calls or opaque control flow conservatively retain the note), over an unfiltered count by the reference or a `sum` by it over a count-like column (reading a count, requiring another key's, or requiring it after the delete or in a branch proves nothing), or a status of `p` that every child insert flips away from; a non-count-like `sum` guard keeps the note and says why. Replaces `N_DELETE_ORPHANS`, retired: no action-carrying reference leaves the rows present at a delete behind unless it says `keep`. |

# LLP 3000.036.002: Intent-aware lints and production bootstrap

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 language diagnostics, CLI fixtures and production readiness, core audience-grant validation and development refusal traces
**Author:** Codex for Charlie Cheever (DIAG)
**Date:** 2026-10-13
**Revised:** 2026-10-14 (QDIAG amendment §§7–8; original implementation 2026-10-13)
**Related:** [LLP 3000.036](./3000.036-what-the-app-farm-asks-of-the-rule-language.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.036.000](./3000.036.000-sharing-shapes-at-sync-and-union-audiences.rfc.md), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) §20.6, [LLP 3000.011.003.000](./3000.011.003.000-offline-ready-application-starters.rfc.md) §8

## 1. Scope and motivation

Wave 10 fix-list #5, #7 and #15 expose disagreement between diagnostics and
execution: deliberate negative fixture tests look stale, one missing roster
produces many writer notes, and the recommended service-seat bootstrap fails
membership-grant validation. The coordinator authorizes this work and its
reviews. HS owns handshake/types, REL2 release lifecycle, DX2 starters/client
recipes, and PAGE2 general planner/rules expressiveness.

## 2. Service seats grant membership on the server

An entire positive branch `viewer = 'service:NAME'` with a nonempty name
satisfies the membership insert/update grant guard. The namespace is reserved:
accounts cannot mint it, and the operator mints a seat token against the
production store. This authority may enroll the first member or retarget a
mutable membership. An `and` branch containing that equality is likewise
restricted to the seat. An `or` requires every branch independently to prove
membership, a newly created group, or a seat. Negation, inequality, arbitrary
principal literals, and ordinary self-join remain insufficient.

Read-rule service grants already disappear before device audience inference
(LLP 3000.007 §20.6); this change adds no sync audience, capability or wire
format. Devices never hold service-seat partitions. Existing seat-session expiry and
revocation checks apply before a mutation is dispatched; no token lifecycle
changes are proposed. Only mintable service names (1–64 ASCII alphanumeric,
underscore or hyphen) qualify for production readiness; an impossible seat
keeps the root's N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER note rather than counting as a writer. Adding membership still
uses ordinary constraints, transactional logs and scope delivery/revocation.
An authenticated seat writes via an ordinary mutation, without system bypass.
The deploy card compiles a synced staff-roster example. The diagnostic's
inline recipe retains an already-online roster's residency and its entire
read predicate; references are resolved in the application's existing schema. Refused ordinary
self-join offers that trusted bootstrap beside the prior-member alternative,
without a redundant `(service and member) or member` rewrite. The offered
seat rule replaces the unsafe branch, never adds a seat to an otherwise
unprotected self-join.

## 3. Readiness notes identify blockers

Keep the positive-dependency least fixed point of LLP 3000.011.003.000 §8.
For blocked tables, dependencies are positive insert-rule probes/reference
terms and a read-through parent. Group mutually dependent tables; report one
note per root blocked component, listing the blocked descendants it governs.
Multiple roots can name a shared dependent. A table with no writer is a root
blocker even if its rule mentions another blocked table. Optional alternatives
remain conservative: this is advice, not a satisfiability/reachability theorem.
Native opaque jobs still prevent an absence claim. A persona-only schema
(with no production identity method) emits one production-identity note,
including the blocked tables when any exist, rather than one per table.

## 4. Fixture intent and guard visibility

`check` inspects shared cases only for operations declared by the checked
schema. Signature/decoding/validation failures whose runtime code is E_INPUT
are intentional when `expect` equals E_INPUT, so emit no argument-drift note.
Malformed JSON/case metadata remain authoring errors. Unknown operations
remain a hard error in `test`, including misspellings; read-only `check`
intentionally cannot distinguish shared newer operations from misspellings.
Expectations are the existing public refusal code, without a new phase field;
the runner prints the E_INPUT message/subcause on each passing negative case
so a negative assertion is reviewable without introducing another note. Saved runtime values
remain unknown. The fixture runner recognizes decoding/signature errors as
E_INPUT too; tests cannot claim success by skipping the case.

A program `exists` sees only caller-readable rows; a table-rule probe has
authority over all rows. Keep those semantics. Add N_UNREADABLE_PROBE on
negative requirements and conditional guards over private populations unless
the pins/preceding guards prove readability. Positive `require exists` is
itself an authorization check; retain its existing N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN analysis.
To cover values later used through aliases/returned helper booleans, inspect
point-existence observations when they happen, as well as mutation/job prefix
observations. Pure query prefix/search/seek values remain ordinary visible-hit
answers; trace guard-consumed bindings through aliases/objects/possible rebinding
and check those observations with their original authority. Inputs to a
guard-consuming helper/shape are conservatively tracked too (all inputs,
rather than an unsound partial parameter-use proof), including direct expression
inputs and guarded loop domains. Row-dependent candidate filters remain intentional
visible-hit query predicates (global gates lower to whole-page conditionals
and are checked); a computed boolean field passed to a guard is
still an observation. Snapshot markers exclude names in every actual argument,
so postorder substitution cannot match a fabricated name in another argument.
Helpers/shapes reached from a requirement inspect every direct input value,
including helpers that only invert it in their returned expression. A direct
positive `require exists` remains authorization. Traverse requirement
wrappers (members, objects, calls and nested conditionals), preserving direct
positive-existence authorization. Inline shape bases are inspected independently
of named-definition discovery. Expand object/write shorthand in analysis copies
before classifying global facts, discovering effects, checking shadowing, or
excluding/substituting/validating argument names; shorthand and explicit names
must have the same scope and proof behavior. Runtime expressions stay unchanged.
Name the target read
rule and explain that negative guards cannot enforce global absence. This is
advisory; an intentionally caller-visible guard can remain unchanged.

Follow reachable helpers, named shapes and spreads. Facts cross helper scopes
only through actual arguments or viewer/literals, with fresh intermediate names
to prevent caller/formal spelling collisions. A read-only helper can receive
snapshot evidence for an expression argument; a writing helper gets only scalar
names. Successful ruled physical-row lookups prove their unambiguous indexed
row exists, omitting extra projected-field tests. Viewer has one meaning in
user text and compiler-generated recipes. Every extra read-rule equality must
resolve; none may be dropped. Never borrow a positional index's visibility
proof from another same-width index. An explicit computed/unknown index never
falls back to another index, and computed keys are not raw column pins.

Dominating facts are invalidated by writes, relevant binding changes or viewer
scope changes; an unanalysed control-flow path never proves authority. Follow
helper/shape effects conservatively through the call graph; cycles prove
nothing. Potentially writing loops cannot carry entry facts across backedges
or export prior role evidence. Binding-only loops invalidate potentially rebound
names at entry/exit, including break/continue paths. An in-loop readable guard
can reacquire proof each iteration. Inspect domain conditionals too. Before
inspecting a compound expression that may write, drop prior facts because a
later operand can observe the changed world. A standalone helper call with
non-writing arguments instead analyzes the helper's statements in order, then
drops caller evidence if it wrote. Stop at return/break/continue. Viewer scopes
inside jobs use caller reads and receive the note; surrounding system job
guards remain exempt. Use a unique/cap/final constraint for an invariant across
hidden rows; do not recommend making private rows public. Avoid duplicate notes
at one guard/target. This never changes execution or admission.

## 5. Refusals and usable rewrites

Invalid mixed positional/named bracket keys get a specific diagnostic before the
misleading id-type error. Name both consistent spellings, using source text
and the resolved key where available. For `!=` inside a probe, explain that
bracket tests are named equalities; use a pinned key with an external predicate
or a bounded program filter. Table rules remain unique point probes. Moving a test to a program filter
changes visibility to caller-readable rows; explain this explicitly.

A nullable upsert result must be checked with `! else CODE` before it supplies
a reference; `! else` refuses and rolls back the whole mutation when null; explain that the write may succeed while its resulting row is
unreadable. Keep upsert result semantics unchanged.

E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT offers a point read by id and a publicly authorized
maintained aggregate beside the existing scoped scan/constraint/job choices.
E_JOB_CURSOR_INDEX leads with stable id order. E_SYNC never repeats an online
only edit already applied; for an inherited read-through conflict it names the
parent audience and copied-column alternative. A union parent ignores read-through children already marked `online only`
when finding a child that inherits its device audience; it names a synced child
and the copied-column alternative. This implements the existing online-only
repair without adding union capture/retention support. Every emitted concrete rewrite
is compiled against its input schema with zero errors, extending the wave-9
golden test. Advisory notes are permitted when the unchanged schema itself
already carries them; snippet guide blocks must remain note-free.

Fixture loading is operator-owned system work: preserve actual constraint
refusals, including named unique codes, rather than remapping to NOT_FOUND.
Attach table/constraint context in fixture-loader text. The same preservation applies to system-job upsert inserts: a system actor
has no hidden target, so its malformed image reports its constraint instead
of NOT_FOUND. Ordinary callers retain hidden-target/collision suppression. Readable unique conflicts return the
key's authored code when readable under the current caller at write time,
in the same transaction; hidden conflicts keep their non-disclosing answer.
Development traces name a failed read-through parent and viewer-hidden lookups
with the read-rule source. Public client refusals never disclose a hidden row
or the private clause. Do not introduce a public E_RULE variant that reveals
whether the parent exists.

## 6. Acceptance and review

Affected tests cover E_INPUT negative cases, older schemas/shared fixtures,
root/cycle readiness grouping, persona collapse, service bootstrap and sync
revocation, rejected untrusted grants, private guard notes and quiet proven
probes, mixed probes, upsert narrowing, inequality guidance, compiled rewrites,
fixture constraint rollback, readable/hidden unique collisions, and private
parent/read-rule evidence. Run affected Rust/guide suites and ref-check; never
the full scripts/test.sh. Save fresh Muse and Codex reviews verbatim under
llp/reviews and revise until both mark the same text READY.

## 7. Wave 11 diagnostic precision (QDIAG)

The nullable-reference lint counts a null value as needing no target lookup.
At `if arg != null`, only the null edge carries that exemption; a lookup on
the other edge must still dominate the write. Join by intersection and clear
facts on rebinding. This changes advisory output only.

Operation parameter defaults remain explicit: declare an optional parameter
and bind `value = arg ?? literal`. Refuse `= literal` with that exact idiom.
A multi-line `require` keeps `else CODE` on its closing line; the refusal names
that line and preserves the author's code. `?` precedes `on delete keep`.

Program absence checks on a row lookup (`t[key] = null`) use the unreadable
probe note too. Raw table-rule probes, including delete rules, still observe
all rows; the note's repair states that distinction. A later owner `require`
after `! else NOT_FOUND` gets a refusal-order note only when every read-rule
branch requires that same owner. A branch admitting staff or another party
can legitimately reach the custom refusal and keeps the note quiet.

A child-owner proof requires equality in every child insert branch, immutable
(or unupdatable) child owner/reference and parent owner, no maintained or
field-owned value, no opaque/job writer, and equal read rules after replacing
only that owner. No other row fields may appear in either rule. A successful
parent lookup (or a direct probe in its returned row projection) supplies the
parent-readable fact. This is advisory analysis of ordinary admitted writes;
trusted system seeds must preserve the declared application invariant. It never

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

A track that passes every gate is a candidate for promotion. A track that
fails the loop bar or acquisition is killed at the clock unless the author
rules otherwise. A mixed result names the gains and the remaining costs, and
the author decides which substrates move into a survivor — which may be
Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000 §13).

**Clock and checkpoints.** Week 1: floors, experience prototype, simulator
skeleton, first publish. Week 2: server core, partition stream, meter,
simulator invariants. Week 3: web replica, outbox, local queries, the chat
app on the real client. Week 4: Expo, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop
bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record. Four weeks from the first
commit.

## 4. Fence, numbering, routing

- **Directory.** `snapback4/`, with its own Cargo workspace and Bun
  workspace, excluded from the root workspace as `snapback2/` is. No
  Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 gate, inventory, backstop, registry or script
  applies; the pre-push hook selects none.
- **Identities.** Binary and npm shim `snapback4`; data directory
  `.snapback4/`; ephemeral ports; its own personas, credentials and process
  ownership. It never depends at runtime on Snapback 2 internals. It copies
  source deliberately, split under the 1,500-line cap on the way in.
- **Numbering.** LLPs 3000 and up; sub-LLPs `3000.NNN`; `llp-create
  --under 3000`. The 2000 series remains Snapback 3's and the 1000 series
  Snapback 2's. Design work for Snapback 4 extends this root or LLP 2000.000
  before it creates a new number.
- **Rules.** `rules/RULES.md` binds "LLPs numbered 1000 and up" by its
  letter, as LLP 2000 §9 noted for its own series. Snapback 4 adopts its
  budgets and loop shape as its own: five blocking checks in sixty seconds,
  `check` under a second, `doctor` under half a second, 1,500 lines per
  file, a bounded working set, delete rather than deprecate, only
  implemented specs bind. Its not-doing list is `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`,
  written with the directory. The root `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` routing line
  and the rules fence note land with the directory, not with this document.
- **Publication.** From week one, `0.0.x`, one script; the benchmark runs
  the published pin.

## 5. The first four weeks

**Week 1 — before any engine.**

- The index-card client API as TypeScript types, and the chat reference
  application written against it with a mocked client; mocks labelled.
- The hub fixture generator, copied from the Clique instrument.
- Five floor measurements recorded in the journal: one gzipped HTTP response
  of 10,000 rows; one IndexedDB transaction writing 10,000 rows; one
  `expo-sqlite` bulk insert of 10,000 rows; a local query over 100,000 rows
  in the TypeScript interpreter and in SQLite; a 50-member send's partition
  fan-out on the server.
- The simulator skeleton: a single-threaded scheduler, a virtual clock, an
  in-process server and N clients, and fault injection (drop, reorder,
  duplicate and delay; failed `fsync`; kill at any await; clock skew).
- The Snapback 2 pieces copied in: rule analysis, Quarry front end, value
  model and corpus, successor classifier.
- 0.0.1 published by day five, even if it serves one query.

**Week 2 — server.** SQLite transaction and interpreter; rules and
uniformity; the meter; the partition log with `sync`, `bootstrap`,
tombstones and receipts, generalised from `deliver`; generations; personas;
the wire with typed states; `check`, `dev`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `guide`;
the seven simulator invariants (§6) running on every commit.

**Week 3 — web client.** The replica store on IndexedDB; the outbox with
client-minted ids; the local interpreter; reactive local queries; the
online-only tier; the chat application on the real client; the Chromium
witness for the boundaries the simulator cannot reach.

**Week 4 — native, day two, comparison.** The Expo client on native SQLite,
with the Rust-core-as-native-module measurement (LLP 2000.000 §9); the
day-two scenarios (additive, widening, narrowing, rule change, rename, each
with a queued write and an old client attached); H0 deploy, rollback and
secrets copied; the loop bar run; the Clique hub run; the comparison record.

The journal records what actually happened against this plan. This section
is not rewritten to match it.

## 6. The simulator's invariants

Pinned here so that no slice can drop one quietly.

1. No silent state: every read is complete, capped, partial or denied;
   every write is pending, sent or failed.
2. A row revoked at sequence S never renders on a client whose watermark
   has passed S.
3. A write with a given id applies at most once.
4. Every prediction converges to the server's state or to a typed conflict.
5. Every screen state is on the card (§2).
6. Watermarks are monotone per partition.
7. After faults stop, every client converges.

## 7. Decisions during execution

Recorded here, dated, as they are made. The provisional answers carried from
LLP 2000.000 §14 until the lead rules: `sync` is explicit, not the default;
a count horizon is required and a time horizon optional; the online-only
tier ships in slice one as the control for every local-first measurement;
the native-module decision follows the week-1 measurement; the effects host
is measured on the four reference effects before it is chosen; ids are a
time-ordered 128-bit form.

- **2026-09-12 — toolchain (author).** `tsc` for typechecking; `rolldown`
  for bundles and the client byte measurement; `oxc` for transforms and
  minification; esbuild only with a reason written in the journal first.
  Snapback 2's measurements are pinned to esbuild 0.25.9 and are therefore
  not byte-comparable with Snapback 4's; the competitor anchors in §3 are
  measured with Snapback 4's own pinned toolchain.
- **2026-09-12 — the simulator is Rust (lead).** `crates/snapback4-sim`
  hosts the Rust server core from week 2. Whether it hosts the client
  through a Rust client core or through an embedded JS engine running the
  TypeScript client is decided with the week-3 byte measurement
  (LLP 2000.000 §9); until then the client is exercised by the simulator's
  witnesses only where its logic is shared with the server.
- **2026-09-12 — the clock started** at the fence commit `961a3eb1c5`.
- **2026-09-12 — Quarry is bound in Rust (lead).** Snapback 2 lowers Quarry

@@ -0,0 +1,56 @@
+//! A fitting recipe for the whole delete, evaluated with the engine's prices.
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, Constraint, references::{OnDelete, Price}};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+
+pub(super) fn reaches(schema:&Schema,root:&str,target:&str)->bool {
+    let mut seen=BTreeSet::from([root.to_owned()]);let mut todo=vec![root.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(parent)=todo.pop() {
+        if parent==target {return true;}
+        for r in schema.referencing(&parent) {if r.action==OnDelete::Cascade && seen.insert(r.child.into()) {todo.push(r.child.into());}}
+    }
+    false
+}
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — caps share one delete reservation, never a per-child allowance.
+pub(super) fn recipe(schema: &Schema, parent: &str, job: bool) -> String {
+    let mut targets = BTreeSet::new();
+    let mut reached = BTreeSet::from([parent.to_owned()]);
+    let mut todo = vec![parent.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(table) = todo.pop() {
+        for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
+            if r.action.writes() { targets.insert((r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned())); }
+            if r.action == OnDelete::Cascade && reached.insert(r.child.to_owned()) { todo.push(r.child.to_owned()); }
+        }
+    }
+    let candidate = |cap: u32| {
+        let mut trial = schema.clone();
+        for (child, column) in &targets {
+            // A unique reference already has the tightest possible bound.
+            if trial.reference_cap(child, column) == Some(1) { continue; }
+            let table = trial.tables.get_mut(child).unwrap();
+            let mut found = false;
+            for constraint in &mut table.constraints {
+                if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint && by.len() == 1 && &by[0] == column {
+                    *max = (*max).min(cap); found = true;
+                }
+            }
+            if !found { table.constraints.push(Constraint::Cap { by: vec![column.clone()], max: cap }); }
+        }
+        trial.prepare_write_reservations();
+        let price = trial.delete_total_reservation(parent)?;
+        Some((trial, price))
+    };
+    let fits = |price: &Price| super::reference_actions::over_ceiling(price, job).is_none();
+    let fallback = format!("keep `on delete restrict` and delete the child rows first in a job (`for row in <child>:`), then delete the {parent} row; choose caps only if they are product limits (guide writes)");
+    if !candidate(1).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { return format!("even cap 1 cannot fit this delete's fixed heap/row reservation; {fallback}"); }
+    let (mut low, mut high) = (1, 500);
+    while low < high {
+        let mid = low + (high - low + 1) / 2;
+        if candidate(mid).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { low = mid; } else { high = mid - 1; }
+    }
+    let (trial, price) = candidate(low).unwrap();
+    let unique=targets.iter().map(|(child,column)|format!("`unique {column}` on {child}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
+    let caps = targets.iter().map(|(child, column)| format!("`cap {} by {column}` on {child}", trial.reference_cap(child, column).unwrap())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
+    let limits = if job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() };
+    format!("apply {caps} together: the whole delete (including the root) reserves {} / {} rows written and {} / {} heap bytes, with all indexes, derived writes and event images priced together. A count-maintained source commonly reserves 3 × its rows (the row plus its old and new aggregate groups), not one row per child; for a one-to-one relation use {unique} instead; {fallback}", price[3], limits.rows_written, price[4], limits.heap_bytes)
+}

@@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
+//! Advisory proof of a child owner copied and checked against a stable parent.
+use super::catalog::Catalog;
+use crate::ast::{self, Expr, ReadExpr, ShapeField};
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, Table, Constraint, ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+fn stable(table: &Table, fields: &[&str]) -> bool {
+    if fields.iter().any(|field|table.written_by.contains_key(*field)||table.maintained.contains_key(*field)) { return false; }
+    matches!(table.rules.update, RuleExpr::Deny) || fields.iter().all(|field| table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Immutable { fields } if fields.iter().any(|f| f == field))))
+}
+fn conjuncts(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> { match rule { RuleExpr::And(rs) => rs.iter().flat_map(conjuncts).collect(), r => vec![r] } }
+fn equality(rule: &RuleExpr, a: &RuleTerm, b: &RuleTerm) -> bool {
+    conjuncts(rule).iter().any(|r| matches!(r, RuleExpr::Eq(x,y) if x == a && y == b || x == b && y == a))
+}
+fn rename(rule: &mut RuleExpr, child: &str, parent: &str) {
+    let term = |t: &mut RuleTerm| if matches!(t, RuleTerm::Field(f) if f == child) { *t = RuleTerm::Field(parent.into()); };
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a); term(b); }
+        RuleExpr::And(rs) | RuleExpr::Or(rs) => for r in rs { rename(r,child,parent); },
+        RuleExpr::Not(r) => rename(r,child,parent),
+        RuleExpr::Exists { key,equalities,.. } | RuleExpr::Created { key,equalities,.. } => { for k in key { term(k); } for (_,v) in equalities { term(v); } },
+        _ => {}
+    }
+}
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — no application assertion alone verifies stored child ownership.
+pub(super) fn readable(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, read: &ReadExpr, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
+    let Some(child) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false; };
+    if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || system_writes(catalog,&read.table) { return false; }
+    for part in &read.keys {
+        let Some(reference) = &part.column else { continue; };
+        let Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: parent_name }) = child.columns.get(reference) else { continue; };
+        let Some(parent) = schema.table(parent_name) else { continue; };
+        if schema.maintain_for(parent_name).is_some() || system_writes(catalog,parent_name) { continue; }
+        let input = crate::print::expression(&part.value);
+        let known_parent = known.contains(&format!("exists {parent_name}[id = {input}]"));
+        let projected_parent = matches!(&part.value, Expr::Member { base,name,.. } if name == "id" && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Focal { .. }))
+            && projected_from(catalog, read, parent_name);
+        if !known_parent && !projected_parent { continue; }
+        for (owner, kind) in &child.columns {
+            if *kind != ColumnType::Principal || !stable(child, &[reference,owner]) { continue; }
+            for (parent_owner, kind) in &parent.columns {
+                if *kind != ColumnType::Principal || !stable(parent, &[parent_owner]) { continue; }
+                let a = RuleTerm::Field(owner.clone());
+                let b = RuleTerm::Reference { table: parent_name.clone(), column: reference.clone(), field: parent_owner.clone(), old:false };
+                let verified = child.rules.insert.disjuncts().iter().all(|branch| equality(branch,&a,&b)
+                    || equality(branch,&a,&RuleTerm::Viewer) && equality(branch,&b,&RuleTerm::Viewer));
+                if !verified || matches!(child.rules.insert,RuleExpr::Deny) { continue; }
+                if child.rules.read.any_term(&|t| matches!(t,RuleTerm::Field(f) if f!=owner)) || parent.rules.read.any_term(&|t| matches!(t,RuleTerm::Field(f) if f!=parent_owner)) { continue; }
+                let mut readers = child.rules.read.clone(); rename(&mut readers,owner,parent_owner);
+                if readers == parent.rules.read { return true; }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    false
+}
+fn system_writes(catalog:&Catalog,table:&str)->bool {
+    catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ast::ProgramKind::Job).any(|p| {
+        if p.native { return true; }
+        let mut changed=false;let mut body=p.prelude.clone();body.extend(p.body.clone());
+        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |e| if matches!(e,Expr::Write { table:t,.. } if t==table) || matches!(e,Expr::Call { .. }) { changed=true; });
+        changed
+    })
+}
+fn projected_from(catalog: &Catalog, wanted: &ReadExpr, parent: &str) -> bool {
+    let mut found = false;
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for p in &doc.programs {
+            let mut body = p.body.clone();
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |expr| {
+                if let Expr::Shape { base,fields,.. } = expr && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Read(r) if r.table == parent) {
+                    // Only the direct field's probe has this focal row. Nested shapes do not.
+                    found |= fields.iter().any(|f| matches!(f,ShapeField::Field { value:Expr::Exists { read,.. },.. } if read.site == wanted.site));
+                }
+            });
+        }
+    }
+    found
+}
+/// Positional typed table arguments denote id, even beside other unique keys.
+pub(super) fn typed_id(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
+    let [part] = read.keys.as_slice() else { return false; };
+    let Expr::Name { name,.. } = &part.value else { return false; };
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for p in &doc.programs {
+            if !p.args.iter().any(|a| a.name == *name && matches!(&a.type_.kind,ast::TypeKind::Ref { table } if table == &read.table)) { continue; }
+            let mut seen = false; let mut body=p.body.clone();
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |e| if matches!(e,Expr::Read(r) if r.site == read.site) { seen=true; });
+            if seen { return true; }
+        }
+    }
+    false
+}

+    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
+        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
+        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
+    }
+
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
+    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
+    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
+        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
+            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
+            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
+                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
+            }
+        }
+        (value, ty)
+    }
+

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..5a29b805b6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,109 @@
+//! Advisory mismatches between a program's caller gate and its table rule.
+use super::catalog::Catalog;
+use crate::{ast::*, Diagnostic};
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+fn seat(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
+    let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. }=expr else { return None; };
+    if op == "and" { return seat(left).or_else(|| seat(right)); }
+    if op != "=" { return None; }
+    let name = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Name { name,.. } if name == "viewer");
+    let value = if name(left) { right.as_ref() } else if name(right) { left.as_ref() } else { return None; };
+    match value { Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(s),.. } if s.starts_with("service:") => Some(s), _=>None }
+}
+fn fields(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<String> {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>vec![f.clone()],
+        RuleExpr::And(rs)|RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().flat_map(fields).collect(),
+        _=>vec![]
+    }
+}
+fn required_owners(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<String> {
+    let branches=rule.disjuncts();
+    let Some(first)=branches.first() else {return vec![]};
+    let mut common=fields(first);
+    for branch in &branches[1..] { let have=fields(branch);common.retain(|f|have.contains(f)); }
+    common
+}
+fn admits_seat(rule: &RuleExpr, seat: &str) -> bool {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Public { .. }=>true,
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>v.as_str().ok()==Some(seat),
+        RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().any(|r|admits_seat(r,seat)),
+        _=>false
+    }
+}
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — these notes state conditional argument requirements, never an impossibility theorem over dynamic inputs.
+pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
+    let mut out=Vec::new();
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for table in &doc.tables {
+            for column in &table.columns {
+                if column.type_.kind==TypeKind::Principal && let Some(Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(value),..})=&column.default
+                    && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p|p==value) {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(&column.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{}.{} defaults to bare persona '{value}', whose dev principal is 'dev:{value}'",table.name,column.name),format!("use `default 'dev:{value}'` for development, or supply the authenticated principal in the mutation for production")).guide("personas"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        for program in &doc.programs {
+            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query || program.native { continue; }
+            body(catalog,schema,program,&program.body,None,&mut out);
+        }
+    }
+    out
+}
+fn body(catalog:&Catalog,schema:&Schema,program:&ProgramDecl,statements:&[Stmt], mut gate:Option<String>,out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
+    let mut lookups: BTreeMap<String,(String,Vec<String>)>=BTreeMap::new();
+    for statement in statements {
+        if let Stmt::Require { condition,code,site,.. }=statement {
+            if let Some(seat)=seat(condition) { gate=Some(seat.into()); }
+            for (alias,(table,owners)) in &lookups {
+                let mut uses_owner=false; let mut copy=condition.clone();
+                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy,&mut |e| if let Expr::Member { base,name,.. }=e && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Name { name,.. } if name==alias) && owners.contains(name) { uses_owner=true; });
+                if uses_owner && code != "NOT_FOUND" {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",format!("{}: callers who cannot read {table} get NOT_FOUND at `{alias}`'s lookup, never {code} from this require",program.name),
+                        format!("put a caller authorization check before the lookup if {code} must win; otherwise keep NOT_FOUND as the privacy-preserving answer and test it (guide rules)")).guide("rules"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if let Stmt::Bind { names,value:Expr::Assert { value,code,.. },.. }=statement && code=="NOT_FOUND" && let Expr::Read(read)=value.as_ref() {
+            if let [name]=names.as_slice() && let Some(table)=schema.table(&read.table) {
+                let owners=required_owners(&table.rules.read);
+                if !owners.is_empty() { lookups.insert(name.clone(),(read.table.clone(),owners)); }
+            }
+        }
+        let mut copy=vec![statement.clone()];
+        // Inspect only this statement's expressions; nested control flow receives its own gate below.
+        if !matches!(statement,Stmt::If { .. }|Stmt::For { .. }|Stmt::As { .. }) {
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy,&mut |expr| {
+                let Expr::Write { op,table,fields:payload,site,.. }=expr else { return; };
+                let Some(def)=schema.table(table) else { return; };
+                for field in payload.iter() {
+                    let principal=catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(&field.name)).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Principal);
+                    if principal && let Some(Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(value),.. })=&field.value
+                        && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p| p==value) {
+                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(&field.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{table}.{} stores bare persona '{value}', but its dev viewer is 'dev:{value}'",field.name),format!("write 'dev:{value}' for development, or an authenticated account principal in production; personas are names, not principals")).guide("personas"));
+                    }
+                }
+                let Some(seat)=gate.as_deref() else { return; };
+                let effect=match op { WriteOp::Insert=>"insert",WriteOp::Update=>"update", _=>return };
+                let rule=if effect=="insert" { &def.rules.insert } else { &def.rules.update };
+                if admits_seat(rule,seat) { return; }
+                let others=fields(rule).into_iter().filter(|owner| payload.iter().find(|f|&f.name==owner).map_or(effect=="update",|f| {
+                    let value=f.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(||Expr::Name { name:f.name.clone(),site:f.site.clone() });
+                    !matches!(value,Expr::Name { ref name,.. } if name=="viewer") && !matches!(value,Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(ref s),.. } if s==seat)
+                })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+                if others.is_empty() { return; }
+                out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_WRITE_DENIED",format!("{} admits only '{seat}', but {table}'s {effect} rule `{}` requires the written {} to be this caller; writing another principal's value refuses E_RULE",program.name,rule.quarry(),others.join(", ")),
+                    format!("if this trusted seat should write other principals' rows, amend `{effect} <- {}` to `{effect} <- ({}) or viewer = '{seat}'`; the mutation require alone grants no table authority (guide deploy, first admin)",rule.quarry(),rule.quarry())).guide("rules"));
+            });
+        }
+        match statement {
+            Stmt::If { condition,then_body,else_body,.. }=> { body(catalog,schema,program,then_body,seat(condition).map(String::from).or_else(||gate.clone()),out); body(catalog,schema,program,else_body,gate.clone(),out); }
+            Stmt::For { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,gate.clone(),out),
+            Stmt::As { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,None,out),
+            Stmt::Return { .. }|Stmt::Break { .. }|Stmt::Continue { .. }=>break,
+            _=>{}
+        }
+    }
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..31869c30ea
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,202 @@
+//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
+use super::Parser;
+use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
+impl Parser {
+    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
+        self.take("(")?;
+        let mut args = Vec::new();
+        while !self.at(")") {
+            let name = self.peek().clone();
+            let binding = self.local_name()?;
+            self.take(":")?;
+            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
+            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
+                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
+                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
+                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
+                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
+                }
+                d
+            })?;
+            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
+                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
+                self.position += 1;
+                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
+                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
+                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
+                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
+            }
+            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
+            if !self.maybe(",") {
+                break;
+            }
+        }
+        self.take(")")?;
+        Ok(args)
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let mut kind;
+        if self.maybe("[") {
+            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
+            self.take("]")?;
+            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
+            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
+            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
+                if parser.at("-") {
+                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
+                }
+                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
+                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
+                if token.value.contains('.') {
+                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
+                }
+                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
+            };
+            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                (0, number(self)?)
+            } else {
+                let min = number(self)?;
+                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
+                (min, number(self)?)
+            };
+            if min > max {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
+            }
+            if item.optional {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
+            }
+            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
+        } else {
+            let name = self.word()?;
+            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
+                "text" => {
+                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                        (None, self.number()?)
+                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
+                        let min = self.number()?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
+                    } else {
+                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
+                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
+                        (None, 10_000)
+                    };
+                    let mut format = None;
+                    if self.maybe("format") {
+                        let value = self.word()?;
+                        if value.value != "handle" && value.value != "url" {
+                            let bound = match min {
+                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
+                                None => format!("<={max}"),
+                            };
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle or text {bound} format url")));
+                        }
+                        format = Some(value.value);
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
+                }
+                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
+                "money" => {
+                    let currency = self.word()?;
+                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
+                    }
+                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
+                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
+                }
+                "json" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "bytes" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "image" => {
+                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
+                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
+                }
+                "video" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
+                }
+                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
+                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
+                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    let max = self.number()?;
+                    let mut types = Vec::new();
+                    if self.maybe("(") {
+                        while !self.at(")") {
+                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
+                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
+                            }
+                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
+                            }
+                            types.push(value.value);
+                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
+                        }
+                        self.take(")")?;
+                        if types.is_empty() {
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
+                        }
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
+                }
+                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
+                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
+                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
+                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
+                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;
+                    TypeKind::IntRange { min, max }
+                }
+                "int" => TypeKind::Int,
+                "time" => TypeKind::Time,
+                "bool" => TypeKind::Bool,
+                "enum" => {
+                    self.take("(")?;
+                    let mut variants = Vec::new();
+                    while !self.at(")") {
+                        variants.push(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value);
+                        if !self.maybe(",") {
+                            break;
+                        }
+                    }
+                    self.take(")")?;
+                    if variants.is_empty() {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&site, "enum needs at least one variant", "enum('draft', 'published')"));
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Enum { variants }
+                }
+                other => TypeKind::Ref { table: other.to_owned() },
+            };
+        }
+        if self.maybe("once") {
+            if !column {
+                let optional = self.maybe("?");
+                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is a column modifier, not an argument constraint; declare it on the owning table column", print::scalar_type(&ScalarType { kind, optional })).mechanical());
+            }
+            match &mut kind {
+                TypeKind::Image { once, .. } => *once = true,
+                TypeKind::Video { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; video ranges are unspecified", "use photo: image <=10000000 once, or remove once from the video column")),
+                TypeKind::File { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; a downloaded file is saved by the browser, so one view cannot be kept", "remove once from the file column; use image <=10000000 once for one-view photos").guide("assets")),
+                _ => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns", "remove once from this column; use image <=10000000 once for one-use images")),
+            }
+        }
+        let optional = self.maybe("?");
+        Ok(ScalarType { kind, optional })
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn media_size(&mut self) -> Fallible<u32> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let size = self.number()?;
+        let multiplier = match self.peek().value.as_str() { "KB" => 1_000, "MB" => 1_000_000, "GB" => 1_000_000_000, _ => return Ok(size) };
+        self.position += 1;
+        size.checked_mul(multiplier).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&site, "upload bound exceeds 4,294,967,295 bytes", "use image <=5MB or a byte count fitting u32").guide("types"))
+    }
+}

+    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
+        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
+        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
+    }
+
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
+    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
+    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
+        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
+            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
+            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
+                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
+            }
+        }
+        (value, ty)
+    }
+

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..d7f37c0772
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,33 @@
+//! Development argument checks shared by the host and fixture runner.
+use serde_json::Value as Json;
+#[cfg(test)] use serde_json::json;
+use snapback4_core::Refusal;
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+pub(crate) fn arguments(personas:&[String],kinds:&BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>,args:&Json)->Option<Refusal> {
+    fn bare(kind:&snapback4_core::ir::ArgType,value:&Json,personas:&[String])->Option<String> {
+        use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
+        match kind {
+            ArgType::Principal => value.as_str().filter(|v| personas.iter().any(|p| p==v)).map(String::from),
+            ArgType::Optional(inner)=>bare(inner,value,personas),
+            ArgType::List { of:Some(inner),.. }|ArgType::ListRange { of:Some(inner),.. }=>value.as_array()?.iter().find_map(|v|bare(inner,v,personas)),
+            _=>None
+        }
+    }
+    let fields=args.as_object()?;
+    kinds.iter().find_map(|(name,kind)|bare(kind,fields.get(name)?,personas).map(|p|Refusal::new("E_INPUT","input",format!("argument {name}: '{p}' is a persona name, not a principal; write 'dev:{p}' (or use the authenticated account principal in production)")).guide("personas")))
+}
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod qdiag_tests {
+ use super::*;
+ use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
+ #[test] fn declared_persona_principals_are_checked_without_rewriting_identity(){
+  let personas=vec!["alice".into()];let kinds=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Principal)]);
+  let value=json!({"p":"alice"});
+  let why=arguments(&personas,&kinds,&value).unwrap();assert_eq!(why.code,"E_INPUT");assert!(why.message.contains("dev:alice"));assert_eq!(value,json!({"p":"alice"}));
+  for p in ["dev:alice","aliceOther","user:alice","service:alice"] {assert!(arguments(&personas,&kinds,&json!({"p":p})).is_none());}
+  assert!(arguments(&[],&kinds,&value).is_none());
+  assert!(arguments(&personas,&BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Id)]),&value).is_none());
+  let lists=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Optional(Box::new(ArgType::List {of:Some(Box::new(ArgType::Principal)),max:5})))]);
+  assert!(arguments(&personas,&lists,&json!({"p":["dev:alice","alice"]})).is_some());
+ }
+}

                 for (name, value) in fields {

+fn bootstrap_recipe(bound: &Bound, table: &str) -> String {
+    let writers: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().filter(|p| ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(target,effect)| target==table && matches!(*effect,"insert"|"upsert"))).map(|p|p.program.name.clone()).collect();
+    let missing = if writers.is_empty() { format!("missing mutation path: no mutation inserts {table}") }
+        else { format!("missing service-seat bootstrap path through {} (their {table} insert rule depends on rows not yet provisioned)",writers.join(", ")) };
+    format!("{missing}; add a bootstrap{table} mutation that requires `viewer = 'service:ops'`, allow that branch in {table}'s insert rule, and make its required probes readable to the seat. Mint `snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment`, then call the mutation with the real account principal and its bearer token. For synced rosters use the independent seat-or-member branch; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin")
+}
