diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..de563fb355
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,56 @@
+//! A fitting recipe for the whole delete, evaluated with the engine's prices.
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, Constraint, references::{OnDelete, Price}};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+
+pub(super) fn reaches(schema:&Schema,root:&str,target:&str)->bool {
+    let mut seen=BTreeSet::from([root.to_owned()]);let mut todo=vec![root.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(parent)=todo.pop() {
+        if parent==target {return true;}
+        for r in schema.referencing(&parent) {if r.action==OnDelete::Cascade && seen.insert(r.child.into()) {todo.push(r.child.into());}}
+    }
+    false
+}
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — caps share one delete reservation, never a per-child allowance.
+pub(super) fn recipe(schema: &Schema, parent: &str, job: bool) -> String {
+    let mut targets = BTreeSet::new();
+    let mut reached = BTreeSet::from([parent.to_owned()]);
+    let mut todo = vec![parent.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(table) = todo.pop() {
+        for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
+            if r.action.writes() { targets.insert((r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned())); }
+            if r.action == OnDelete::Cascade && reached.insert(r.child.to_owned()) { todo.push(r.child.to_owned()); }
+        }
+    }
+    let candidate = |cap: u32| {
+        let mut trial = schema.clone();
+        for (child, column) in &targets {
+            // A unique reference already has the tightest possible bound.
+            if trial.reference_cap(child, column) == Some(1) { continue; }
+            let table = trial.tables.get_mut(child).unwrap();
+            let mut found = false;
+            for constraint in &mut table.constraints {
+                if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint && by.len() == 1 && &by[0] == column {
+                    *max = (*max).min(cap); found = true;
+                }
+            }
+            if !found { table.constraints.push(Constraint::Cap { by: vec![column.clone()], max: cap }); }
+        }
+        trial.prepare_write_reservations();
+        let price = trial.delete_total_reservation(parent)?;
+        Some((trial, price))
+    };
+    let fits = |price: &Price| super::reference_actions::over_ceiling(price, job).is_none();
+    let fallback = format!("keep `on delete restrict` and delete the child rows first in a job (`for row in <child>:`), then delete the {parent} row; choose caps only if they are product limits (guide writes)");
+    if !candidate(1).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { return format!("even cap 1 cannot fit this delete's fixed heap/row reservation; {fallback}"); }
+    let (mut low, mut high) = (1, 500);
+    while low < high {
+        let mid = low + (high - low + 1) / 2;
+        if candidate(mid).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { low = mid; } else { high = mid - 1; }
+    }
+    let (trial, price) = candidate(low).unwrap();
+    let unique=targets.iter().map(|(child,column)|format!("`unique {column}` on {child}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
+    let caps = targets.iter().map(|(child, column)| format!("`cap {} by {column}` on {child}", trial.reference_cap(child, column).unwrap())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
+    let limits = if job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() };
+    format!("apply {caps} together: the whole delete (including the root) reserves {} / {} rows written and {} / {} heap bytes, with all indexes, derived writes and event images priced together. A count-maintained source commonly reserves 3 × its rows (the row plus its old and new aggregate groups), not one row per child; for a one-to-one relation use {unique} instead; {fallback}", price[3], limits.rows_written, price[4], limits.heap_bytes)
+}

+        for note in &mut notes {
+            if matches!(note.code.as_str(),"N_NO_HORIZON"|"N_HORIZON_GUARD"|"N_SYNC_ORDER"|"N_NOT_PREDICTABLE"|"N_HELD_UNKNOWN") {
+                note.message.insert_str(0,"provisional while E_SYNC is unresolved: ");
+            }
+        }
+    }
     // @ref LLP 3000.032#4.3 — use the expanded mapping, never guess default operation names.
     let receipt_members = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| {
         let module = std::path::Path::new(&doc.file).file_stem().unwrap().to_string_lossy();
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..5cb54d03be
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,91 @@
+//! Advisory proof of a child owner copied and checked against a stable parent.
+use super::catalog::Catalog;
+use crate::ast::{self, Expr, ReadExpr, ShapeField};
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, Table, Constraint, ColumnType, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+fn stable(table: &Table, fields: &[&str]) -> bool {
+    if fields.iter().any(|field|table.written_by.contains_key(*field)||table.maintained.contains_key(*field)) { return false; }
+    matches!(table.rules.update, RuleExpr::Deny) || fields.iter().all(|field| table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Immutable { fields } if fields.iter().any(|f| f == field))))
+}
+fn conjuncts(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> { match rule { RuleExpr::And(rs) => rs.iter().flat_map(conjuncts).collect(), r => vec![r] } }
+fn equality(rule: &RuleExpr, a: &RuleTerm, b: &RuleTerm) -> bool {
+    conjuncts(rule).iter().any(|r| matches!(r, RuleExpr::Eq(x,y) if x == a && y == b || x == b && y == a))
+}
+fn rename(rule: &mut RuleExpr, child: &str, parent: &str) {
+    let term = |t: &mut RuleTerm| if matches!(t, RuleTerm::Field(f) if f == child) { *t = RuleTerm::Field(parent.into()); };
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a); term(b); }
+        RuleExpr::And(rs) | RuleExpr::Or(rs) => for r in rs { rename(r,child,parent); },
+        RuleExpr::Not(r) => rename(r,child,parent),
+        RuleExpr::Exists { key,equalities,.. } | RuleExpr::Created { key,equalities,.. } => { for k in key { term(k); } for (_,v) in equalities { term(v); } },
+        _ => {}
+    }
+}
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — no application assertion alone verifies stored child ownership.
+pub(super) fn readable(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, read: &ReadExpr, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
+    let Some(child) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false; };
+    if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || system_writes(catalog,&read.table) { return false; }
+    for part in &read.keys {
+        let Some(reference) = &part.column else { continue; };
+        let Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: parent_name }) = child.columns.get(reference) else { continue; };
+        let Some(parent) = schema.table(parent_name) else { continue; };
+        if schema.maintain_for(parent_name).is_some() || system_writes(catalog,parent_name) { continue; }
+        let input = crate::print::expression(&part.value);
+        let known_parent = known.contains(&format!("exists {parent_name}[id = {input}]"));
+        let projected_parent = matches!(&part.value, Expr::Member { base,name,.. } if name == "id" && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Focal { .. }))
+            && projected_from(catalog, read, parent_name);
+        if !known_parent && !projected_parent { continue; }
+        for (owner, kind) in &child.columns {
+            if *kind != ColumnType::Principal || !stable(child, &[reference,owner]) { continue; }
+            for (parent_owner, kind) in &parent.columns {
+                if *kind != ColumnType::Principal || !stable(parent, &[parent_owner]) { continue; }
+                let a = RuleTerm::Field(owner.clone());
+                let b = RuleTerm::Reference { table: parent_name.clone(), column: reference.clone(), field: parent_owner.clone(), old:false };
+                let verified = child.rules.insert.disjuncts().iter().all(|branch| equality(branch,&a,&b)
+                    || equality(branch,&a,&RuleTerm::Viewer) && equality(branch,&b,&RuleTerm::Viewer));
+                if !verified || matches!(child.rules.insert,RuleExpr::Deny) { continue; }
+                if child.rules.read.any_term(&|t| matches!(t,RuleTerm::Field(f) if f!=owner)) || parent.rules.read.any_term(&|t| matches!(t,RuleTerm::Field(f) if f!=parent_owner)) { continue; }
+                let mut readers = child.rules.read.clone(); rename(&mut readers,owner,parent_owner);
+                if readers == parent.rules.read { return true; }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    false
+}
+fn system_writes(catalog:&Catalog,table:&str)->bool {
+    catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ast::ProgramKind::Job).any(|p| {
+        if p.native { return true; }
+        let mut changed=false;let mut body=p.prelude.clone();body.extend(p.body.clone());
+        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |e| if matches!(e,Expr::Write { table:t,.. } if t==table) || matches!(e,Expr::Call { .. }) { changed=true; });
+        changed
+    })
+}
+fn projected_from(catalog: &Catalog, wanted: &ReadExpr, parent: &str) -> bool {
+    let mut found = false;
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for p in &doc.programs {
+            let mut body = p.body.clone();
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |expr| {
+                if let Expr::Shape { base,fields,.. } = expr && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Read(r) if r.table == parent) {
+                    // Only the direct field's probe has this focal row. Nested shapes do not.
+                    found |= fields.iter().any(|f| matches!(f,ShapeField::Field { value:Expr::Exists { read,.. },.. } if read.site == wanted.site));
+                }
+            });
+        }
+    }
+    found
+}
+/// Positional typed table arguments denote id, even beside other unique keys.
+pub(super) fn typed_id(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
+    let [part] = read.keys.as_slice() else { return false; };
+    let Expr::Name { name,.. } = &part.value else { return false; };
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for p in &doc.programs {
+            if !p.args.iter().any(|a| a.name == *name && matches!(&a.type_.kind,ast::TypeKind::Ref { table } if table == &read.table)) { continue; }
+            let mut seen = false; let mut body=p.body.clone();
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |e| if matches!(e,Expr::Read(r) if r.site == read.site) { seen=true; });
+            if seen { return true; }
+        }
+    }
+    false
+}

     let value_type = match &maintain.column {

         Some(column) => match source.columns.get(column) {
+            Some(ty) if ty.optional && matches!(ty.kind, TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. } | TypeKind::Decimal { .. } | TypeKind::Money { .. }) => {

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..5a29b805b6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,109 @@
+//! Advisory mismatches between a program's caller gate and its table rule.
+use super::catalog::Catalog;
+use crate::{ast::*, Diagnostic};
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+fn seat(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
+    let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. }=expr else { return None; };
+    if op == "and" { return seat(left).or_else(|| seat(right)); }
+    if op != "=" { return None; }
+    let name = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Name { name,.. } if name == "viewer");
+    let value = if name(left) { right.as_ref() } else if name(right) { left.as_ref() } else { return None; };
+    match value { Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(s),.. } if s.starts_with("service:") => Some(s), _=>None }
+}
+fn fields(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<String> {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>vec![f.clone()],
+        RuleExpr::And(rs)|RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().flat_map(fields).collect(),
+        _=>vec![]
+    }
+}
+fn required_owners(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<String> {
+    let branches=rule.disjuncts();
+    let Some(first)=branches.first() else {return vec![]};
+    let mut common=fields(first);
+    for branch in &branches[1..] { let have=fields(branch);common.retain(|f|have.contains(f)); }
+    common
+}
+fn admits_seat(rule: &RuleExpr, seat: &str) -> bool {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Public { .. }=>true,
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>v.as_str().ok()==Some(seat),
+        RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().any(|r|admits_seat(r,seat)),
+        _=>false
+    }
+}
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — these notes state conditional argument requirements, never an impossibility theorem over dynamic inputs.
+pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
+    let mut out=Vec::new();
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for table in &doc.tables {
+            for column in &table.columns {
+                if column.type_.kind==TypeKind::Principal && let Some(Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(value),..})=&column.default
+                    && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p|p==value) {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(&column.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{}.{} defaults to bare persona '{value}', whose dev principal is 'dev:{value}'",table.name,column.name),format!("use `default 'dev:{value}'` for development, or supply the authenticated principal in the mutation for production")).guide("personas"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        for program in &doc.programs {
+            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query || program.native { continue; }
+            body(catalog,schema,program,&program.body,None,&mut out);
+        }
+    }
+    out
+}
+fn body(catalog:&Catalog,schema:&Schema,program:&ProgramDecl,statements:&[Stmt], mut gate:Option<String>,out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
+    let mut lookups: BTreeMap<String,(String,Vec<String>)>=BTreeMap::new();
+    for statement in statements {
+        if let Stmt::Require { condition,code,site,.. }=statement {
+            if let Some(seat)=seat(condition) { gate=Some(seat.into()); }
+            for (alias,(table,owners)) in &lookups {
+                let mut uses_owner=false; let mut copy=condition.clone();
+                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy,&mut |e| if let Expr::Member { base,name,.. }=e && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Name { name,.. } if name==alias) && owners.contains(name) { uses_owner=true; });
+                if uses_owner && code != "NOT_FOUND" {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",format!("{}: callers who cannot read {table} get NOT_FOUND at `{alias}`'s lookup, never {code} from this require",program.name),
+                        format!("put a caller authorization check before the lookup if {code} must win; otherwise keep NOT_FOUND as the privacy-preserving answer and test it (guide rules)")).guide("rules"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if let Stmt::Bind { names,value:Expr::Assert { value,code,.. },.. }=statement && code=="NOT_FOUND" && let Expr::Read(read)=value.as_ref() {
+            if let [name]=names.as_slice() && let Some(table)=schema.table(&read.table) {
+                let owners=required_owners(&table.rules.read);
+                if !owners.is_empty() { lookups.insert(name.clone(),(read.table.clone(),owners)); }
+            }
+        }
+        let mut copy=vec![statement.clone()];
+        // Inspect only this statement's expressions; nested control flow receives its own gate below.
+        if !matches!(statement,Stmt::If { .. }|Stmt::For { .. }|Stmt::As { .. }) {
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy,&mut |expr| {
+                let Expr::Write { op,table,fields:payload,site,.. }=expr else { return; };
+                let Some(def)=schema.table(table) else { return; };
+                for field in payload.iter() {
+                    let principal=catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(&field.name)).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Principal);
+                    if principal && let Some(Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(value),.. })=&field.value
+                        && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p| p==value) {
+                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(&field.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{table}.{} stores bare persona '{value}', but its dev viewer is 'dev:{value}'",field.name),format!("write 'dev:{value}' for development, or an authenticated account principal in production; personas are names, not principals")).guide("personas"));
+                    }
+                }
+                let Some(seat)=gate.as_deref() else { return; };
+                let effect=match op { WriteOp::Insert=>"insert",WriteOp::Update=>"update", _=>return };
+                let rule=if effect=="insert" { &def.rules.insert } else { &def.rules.update };
+                if admits_seat(rule,seat) { return; }
+                let others=fields(rule).into_iter().filter(|owner| payload.iter().find(|f|&f.name==owner).map_or(effect=="update",|f| {
+                    let value=f.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(||Expr::Name { name:f.name.clone(),site:f.site.clone() });
+                    !matches!(value,Expr::Name { ref name,.. } if name=="viewer") && !matches!(value,Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(ref s),.. } if s==seat)
+                })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+                if others.is_empty() { return; }
+                out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_WRITE_DENIED",format!("{} admits only '{seat}', but {table}'s {effect} rule `{}` requires the written {} to be this caller; writing another principal's value refuses E_RULE",program.name,rule.quarry(),others.join(", ")),
+                    format!("if this trusted seat should write other principals' rows, amend `{effect} <- {}` to `{effect} <- ({}) or viewer = '{seat}'`; the mutation require alone grants no table authority (guide deploy, first admin)",rule.quarry(),rule.quarry())).guide("rules"));
+            });
+        }
+        match statement {
+            Stmt::If { condition,then_body,else_body,.. }=> { body(catalog,schema,program,then_body,seat(condition).map(String::from).or_else(||gate.clone()),out); body(catalog,schema,program,else_body,gate.clone(),out); }
+            Stmt::For { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,gate.clone(),out),
+            Stmt::As { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,None,out),
+            Stmt::Return { .. }|Stmt::Break { .. }|Stmt::Continue { .. }=>break,
+            _=>{}
+        }
+    }
+}

codex
The lowering uses a same-table `.id` projection without adding a lookup, and helper calls retain their row snapshots. I’m now checking key resolution, lint proof boundaries, and whether all relevant public E_RULE paths preserve the documented ambiguity.

use snapback4_lang::compile;
fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound { compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]) }
const REFS: &str = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents ? on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n";
#[test]
fn optional_reference_checks_join_the_null_edge() {
    for condition in ["parent != null", "not (parent = null)", "null != parent"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?):\n  if {condition}:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
    for body in ["  if parent != null:\n    unused = true\n", "  if parent != null and skip:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?, other: parents ?, skip: bool):\n{body}  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
}
#[test]
fn syntax_repairs_name_the_actual_form() {
    let b = checked("mutation add(body: text <=20 = 'hi'):\n  return body\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("body ?? 'hi'") && d.rewrite.contains("text <=20 ?")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("mutation add(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n  else NOPE\n  return true\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("closing line") && d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("table parents:\n  public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep ?\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite == "parent: parents ? on delete keep"), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn whole_cascade_recipe_fits_once_across_children() {
    let source="table roots:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable votes:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=10000\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\nmutation remove(root: roots):\n  delete roots[root]\n";
    for source in [source.to_owned(),source.replace("  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:","  owner: principal\n  cap 500 by root\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:").replace("  body: text <=10000\n","  body: text <=10000\n  cap 500 by root\n")] {
        let b=checked(&source);
        let d=b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d|d.code=="E_CASCADE_BOUND").unwrap_or_else(||panic!("{:?}",b.diagnostics));
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains("heap bytes") && d.rewrite.contains("3 ×"),"{}",d.rewrite);
        let caps: Vec<_>=d.rewrite.split('`').filter(|s|s.starts_with("cap ")).collect();
        assert_eq!(caps.len(),2,"{}",d.rewrite);
        let edited=source.lines().filter(|line|!line.trim().starts_with("cap 500")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
        let edited=edited.replace("  root: roots on delete cascade",&format!("  root: roots on delete cascade\n  {}",caps[0]));
        let fixed=checked(&(edited+"\n"));
        assert!(fixed.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{}\n{:?}",d.rewrite,fixed.diagnostics);
        let price=fixed.schema.delete_total_reservation("roots").unwrap();
        let ceiling=snapback4_core::Ceilings::default();
        assert!(price[3]<=ceiling.rows_written && price[4]<=ceiling.heap_bytes,"{price:?}");
    }
}
const COERCION: &str="table parents:\n  name: text <=20\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\nfn label(p: parents): p.name\nquery parentName(p: parents):\n  return parents[p] { name }\nmutation add(id: id):\n  p = insert parents { id, name: 'new' }\n  insert kids { id: 'child', parent: p }\n  return { label: label(p), key: parents[p] }\n";
#[test]
fn contextual_row_ids_preserve_helper_snapshots_and_explicit_ir() {
    let implicit=checked(COERCION);
    assert!(implicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",implicit.diagnostics);
    let explicit=checked(&COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: p.id }").replace("key: parents[p]","key: parents[p.id]"));
    assert!(explicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",explicit.diagnostics);
    fn erase_sites(v:&mut serde_json::Value) { match v { serde_json::Value::Object(o)=> { o.remove("site"); for v in o.values_mut(){erase_sites(v);} }, serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{erase_sites(v);},_=>{} } }
    let programs=|b:&snapback4_lang::Bound| {let mut v=serde_json::to_value(b.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).unwrap();erase_sites(&mut v);v};
    assert_eq!(programs(&implicit),programs(&explicit));
    for source in [COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: (parents[id]) }"),COERCION.replace("id: 'child', parent: p","id: p, parent: p"),COERCION.replace("table kids:","table strangers:\n  public\ntable kids:").replace("parent: parents on delete keep","parent: strangers on delete keep")] {
        let refused=checked(&source); assert!(!refused.diagnostics.is_empty(),"unexpectedly accepted: {source}");
    }
}

    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image <=5GB\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.message.contains("4,294,967,295")));
    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.rewrite.contains("image <=5MB")));
    let b=checked("table values:\n  owner: principal\n  n: int 1..100 ?\n  public\nview totals: sum values of .n by .owner\n");

    let b=checked("table names:\n  name: text <=10\n  public\nmutation add(name: text <=10):\n  insert names { name: lower(name) }\n");

use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
fn text(s:&str)->Value{Value::String(s.into())}
fn args(v:&[(&str,Value)])->BTreeMap<String,Value>{v.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.to_string(),v.clone())).collect()}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
fn why(e:&mut Engine,op:&str,who:&str,values:&[(&str,Value)])->snapback4_core::Refusal{match e.mutate(op,None,Context::new(who,args(values)).at(100)).unwrap().state{WriteState::Failed {why,..}=>why,s=>panic!("{s:?}")}}
const UNIQUE:&str="table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  writer: principal\n  handle: text <=20\n  other: text <=20 ?\n  unique handle\n  unique other else OTHER_TAKEN 'Other key is taken'\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .writer = viewer\n  update <- .writer = viewer\nmutation add(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other}\nmutation absent(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other} if absent\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return rows first 10 by .id after c\n";
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>){e.seed(vec![("rows".into(),snapback4_core::value::object([("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]))]).unwrap();}
fn input(id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>)->Vec<(&'static str,Value)>{vec![("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]}
#[test]
fn visible_unique_names_the_declaration_and_authored_code_wins(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","alice","key",Some("other"));
 let visible=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","key",None));
 assert_eq!(visible.code,"E_UNIQUE");assert!(visible.message.contains("unique handle") && !visible.message.contains("$intent"));
 let named=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","different",Some("other")));assert_eq!(named.code,"OTHER_TAKEN");
 assert_eq!(why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("old","alice","different",None)).code,"E_CONSTRAINT");
 let absent=e.mutate("absent",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input("new","alice","key",None))).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(absent.state,WriteState::Sent {result:Value::Null,..}));
 // Null secondary keys do not conflict; the handle remains independently unique.
 for (i,handle) in [("two","two"),("three","three")] {assert!(matches!(e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input(i,"alice",handle,None))).at(100)).unwrap().state,WriteState::Sent {..}));}
}
#[test]
fn hidden_unique_is_the_same_public_refusal_as_rule_denial_including_seats(){
 for viewer in ["alice","service:ops"] {
  let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","bob","key",None);
  let collision=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&input("new",viewer,"key",None));
  let mut denied=input("new",viewer,"different",None);denied[2].1=id("other");
  let denial=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&denied);
  assert_eq!(collision,denial,"{viewer}");assert_eq!(collision.code,"E_RULE");assert!(collision.message.contains("a row you cannot read holds this key"));
  if viewer.starts_with("service:"){assert!(collision.message.contains("rows rule clause: insert <- .writer = viewer"));}
  assert_eq!(e.store().rows("rows").unwrap().len(),1);
 }
}

fn hidden_counter_is_request_local_and_changes_no_query_envelope_or_meter(){

mod common;
use common::*;
use snapback4_core::{Context, WriteState};
use snapback4_device::{device::Device,store::DeviceStore};
use serde_json::json;
const SOURCE:&str="table parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  unique parent\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nfn label(p: parents): p.label\nmutation add(id: id, child: id, title: text <=20):\n  p = insert parents { id, label: title }\n  insert kids { id: child, parent: p }\n  return label(p)\nmutation clash(id: id, parent: parents):\n  insert parents { id: 'rolledback', label: 'temporary' }\n  insert kids { id, parent }\nquery parents(c: cursor ?):\n  return parents first 20 by .id after c\n";
fn run<H:Host>(host:&mut H){
 let (mut e,b)=compile(SOURCE);
 let mut d=host.open("qdiag",Some(b)).unwrap();
 for (i,page) in snapshot_pages(&mut e,"alice",10).iter().enumerate(){d.apply(page,i==0).unwrap();}
 let args=json!({"id":"p","child":"k","title":"label"});
 d.predict("add","alice",&args,100,vec![],1).unwrap();
 let actual=e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("p")),("child",id("k")),("title",snapback4_core::Value::String("label".into()))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(actual.state,WriteState::Sent { result:snapback4_core::Value::String(ref s),.. } if s=="label"),"{actual:?}");
 let row=e.store().rows("kids").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
 assert_eq!(row.field("parent").unwrap(),&id("p"));
 let predicted=d.predict("clash","alice",&json!({"id":"other","parent":"p"}),100,vec![],2).unwrap_err();
 assert_eq!(predicted.code(),"E_UNIQUE","{predicted:?}");
 let conflict=e.mutate("clash",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("other")),("parent",id("p"))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(conflict.state,WriteState::Failed { ref why,.. } if why.code=="E_UNIQUE" && why.message.contains("unique parent") && !why.message.contains("$intent")),"{conflict:?}");
 assert_eq!(e.store().rows("parents").unwrap().len(),1,"the earlier write rolled back");
 assert_eq!(query(&mut d),json!([{"id":"p","label":"label","pending":true}]),"device refusal rolled back");
 host.close("qdiag",d);
}
fn query<S:DeviceStore>(d:&mut Device<S>)->serde_json::Value{snapback4_device::call::call(d,json!({"op":"query","name":"parents","viewer":"alice","args":{"c":null},"now":100}))["ok"]["data"].clone()}

#[test]fn qdiag_sqlite(){run(&mut Sqlite::new("wave11-qdiag"));}
//! Compare implicit/explicit row-reference worlds through the simulator's
//! wire, receipts, stream, settlement and device-reopen observation harness.
#[path="common/write_observation.rs"] mod observation;
use snapback4_core::Engine;
use serde_json::json;
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
#[test]
fn contextual_projection_preserves_every_published_observation(){
 let source="table parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nmutation create(id: id, kid: id):\n  parent = insert parents { id, label:'parent' }\n  insert kids { id:kid, parent }\n  return parent.id\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return kids first 10 by .id after c\n";
 let mut a=engine(source);let mut b=engine(&source.replace("id:kid, parent","id:kid, parent:parent.id"));
 let (_,a)=observation::observe(&mut a,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 let (_,b)=observation::observe(&mut b,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 a.assert_same(&b);
}
use snapback4_difftest::{generate,execute,lean};
#[test]
fn generated_contextual_sources_match_native_and_lean(){
    for seed in 0..32 {
        let (case,implicit,explicit)=generate::qdiag(seed);
        let a=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&implicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        let b=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&explicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        assert_eq!(a,b,"seed {seed}");
        for source in [&implicit,&explicit] {
            let checked=lean::compare(&case,source).unwrap();
            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict,snapback4_difftest::oracle::Verdict::Pass),"seed {seed}: {:?}",checked.verdict);
        }
    }
}

+/// Wave-11 contextual reference family. Keep the sources independent of IR;
+/// the explicit spelling is a metamorphic oracle for the contextual spelling.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — inserted/lookup snapshots project only in reference contexts.
+pub fn qdiag(seed:u64) -> (Case,String,String) {
+    let mut case=base(seed);
+    case.program.body=Body::Block(Block { stmts:vec![],ret:Expr::Bool(true) });
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear(); case.budget=Ceilings::default();
+    case.args=json!({"key":"new-parent","k2":"new-child","bucket":0,"c":null,"n":null,"a":0,"b":1});
+    let mut rng=Rng::new(seed);
+    let (owner,visible)=(if rng.pick(2)==0 {"alice"} else {"bob"},rng.pick(2)==0);
+    if rng.pick(3)==0 { case.rows.push(("kids".into(),json!({"id":"old-child","item":"new-parent","owner":owner}))); }
+    let read=if visible {"allow"} else {".owner = viewer"};
+    let implicit=format!("table items:\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=64\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  item: items on delete keep\n  owner: principal\n  unique item\n  read <- {read}\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation run(key: id, k2: id, bucket: int, c: cursor ?, n: int ?, a: int, b: int):\n  item = insert items {{ id:key, owner:viewer, label:'parent' }}\n  insert kids {{ id:k2, item, owner:viewer }}\n  return item.id\nquery observe(key: id, k2: id):\n  return {{ item:items[key], child:kids[k2] }}\n");
+    let explicit=implicit.replace("id:k2, item, owner","id:k2, item:item.id, owner");
+    (case,implicit,explicit)
+}

The 0.2.6 Astra lane recorded 25 loops: 2 Snapback defects, 23 agent.
Grok recorded a capability failure at M3 and Muse was at M1 when this
revision was written. Reading every lane's loops by cause rather than by
the lane's honest self-blame:

| Cause | Astra 0.2.6 | Grok 0.2.6 | Muse 0.2.6 (M1, in flight) | Also |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Language cold-start tax: ambiguous index, `where` refused, wrong index name, exact sync audience, module schedule reference, chunk at top level only, null narrowing, a missing `id` tie-break on a cursor index | 5 | "locals bind once … a cold-start tax"; `windows['editMs']` ambiguous until `by`; jobs must start with `chunk`; `public` not accepted on a maintain | — | 0.1.0 M8: four knowledge gaps |
| Spelling the audience twice: the read rule and the `sync` line must pair exactly | 1 | "the friction is rule/sync pairing, not missing primitives" | **six compile iterations** on `E_SYNC` ("my created-members branch was self-justifying") | — |
| Counters written by one principal into another's row collide with a row the writer cannot read (`E_RULE`) | — | **three loops** (`unreads`, `digestAcc`, the digest hit tables); fix: `read <- allow` on four tables | **eight iterations**; fix: two tables made `public`, with the reason string "digest counters are readable so cross-principal upserts collide readably" | — |
| Prediction certificate: a redundant `exists messages[id]` guard beyond the horizon silently made `send` unpredictable | 1 | — | — | — |
| Shaped rows drop device `pending` metadata | 1 | — | — | — |
| A mutation's result does not reach `useMutation().run()` | — | the DM-reuse id could not be trusted; a point query instead | — | — |
| Identity admission limits under a four-persona harness and the scale replay | 2 | yes | — | — |
| `upsert` replaced a row (the avatar) | 1 | — | — | — |
| Harness, React Native Web, Expo/Hermes toolchain, choreography, the app's own bugs | 14 | most of M3 | ffmpeg, kit drift, a dedupe namespace | every lane on every backend |

Two of those rows are new in this revision and change its shape. The
audience spelled twice and the counters another principal owns are not
program-language constraints; they are places where the model asks the
author to restate a fact the engine already has, or refuses a write the
write rule admits. Both builders that stopped, stopped there. Muse's
reason string is the record's plainest sentence: a builder weakening
authorization to satisfy a mechanism.

### 2.3 What the builders wrote

- Astra on 0.1.0: *"there was no ordinary server function into which I
  could move the awkward part … I could not simply write familiar
  collection and arithmetic code when a feature became complicated."*
- Astra on 0.2.6: *"With SQL or ordinary transaction code, I would have
  more familiar filtering and aggregation options immediately available.
  Quarry instead asks me to choose indexes, narrow optional values and
  express bounded work in its own forms."* And, on the fan-out loop the
  compiler refused: *"That was friction, but it also prevented a casual
  implementation from hiding an arbitrary truncation or an uncontrolled
  write fan-out."*
- Grok on 0.2.6: *"Snapback 4 is the right shape for Clique's backend …
  It is not yet the path of least resistance for Clique's web
  choreography."* And: *"The client cannot trust a minted conversation
  id when the mutation reused `dmIndex`. That is a real platform gap
  relative to Convex mutations, which return values into the UI."*
- Astra on Convex: *"The larger missing subsystem was durable offline
  behavior … I wrote an outbox, stable mutation identifiers,
  account-scoped caches, media-file persistence, connectivity tracking,
  and cache cleanup."* Grok on Convex: *"Auth is not a product … Queries
  do not tick … Indexes and scans are your fan-out policy."*
- Astra on Supabase: *"backend choice redistributes engineering work
  rather than making all categories of work vanish."*
- Muse on Snapback 2, the sentence this revision's `data --sql` answers:
  *"when the sync layer's contract surprises you, there is no underlying
  Postgres to query for ground truth."*

### 2.4 The three-backend scores (Snapback 2, 2026-09-05)

Convex 81, Snapback 2 66, Supabase 62 overall. Snapback 2 won
authorization (82 against 79) and offline (81 against 44); it lost
queries and live (60 against 85), client integration (59 against 79),
setup and docs (55 against 79), operations (53 against 76), scale (62
against 80). Supabase's best dimension anywhere was data modeling (88):
SQL is the vocabulary every model already has. These are Snapback 2
numbers; Snapback 4 has since closed docs (the compiled guide), media
(typed assets), background work (jobs, schedules, effects) and the
acquisition floor. The 0.2.6 holistic scores are pending.

## 3. Analysis

### 3.1 Quarry's constraints come from four sources, and only one is the device

| Source | Constraints it explains | Needed when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **D — device execution and prediction.** A program may run on a device over the rows it holds and predict a mutation before the server judges it | reads over synced tables only for a local answer; `connected` unknown on a device; no clock beyond `now`, no randomness, no network; a mutation's fan-out must be a complete capped group so a prediction is exact | the program is meant to run locally |
| **A — authorization is static.** Rules are the audience a partition can hold; completeness is a class the prefix decides | index-prefixed reads; the rule/sync pairing; `exists` over an index; reads never raise a rule refusal | always: this is what Snapback wins on |
| **M — resources are metered.** Examined rows, probes, steps, heap, output and rows written have ceilings (LLP 2000.000 §6) | a mutation aborts `E_BOUND`, a query caps; jobs chunk | always: the process is scale-to-zero and shared |
| **S — Snapback 2's static pricing**, retired by LLP 2000.000 flip 1 but never removed from the binder | a local binds once; no accumulators; no `continue`; no `where`; `range` literals only; no text `+`; the `fn`/`proc` split; the page-to-list gap; `divmod` in place of `/` | never, under the meter |

The author's thesis attributes the constraints to D. The record attributes
the *costly* ones to S, and the ones that stopped the weaker builders to a
fifth thing: **R — restatement**, where the model asks the author to say
a fact twice (the audience, in the rule and in `sync`) or to restate an
authority it already granted (the write rule) as a read grant. LLP
2000.000 §6 named S's outcome in advance — "What it dissolves: … the
`fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap, which existed to keep
collections priceable" — and §4.1 asked that the restrictions be
re-derived once the bound model was decided. They were not; the binder
came over with Snapback 2's refusals intact. R was not in the brief
because Snapback 2 had neither `sync` nor the hidden-collision ruling.

### 3.2 The interpreter already runs what the binder refuses

- `crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/runner.rs`: `Assign` rebinds a local
  (line 149), `Push` appends to a list local under the heap meter (157),
  `Continue` flows (252), `Concat` joins text or lists (417).

//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.is_identity().then_some(ty)
}
//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            let expected = if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
                else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) }
                else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
            ty.comparable(&expected) || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(),expected.required()),(Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a),Ty::Ref(b)) if a==b)) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
        })).collect()
}

pub(super) fn refusal(info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, candidates: &[&IndexDecl], catalog: &Catalog) -> Diagnostic {
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));
        if read.index.is_none() { changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::NamedKey { columns: index.columns.clone(), index: duplicate.then(|| index.name.clone()) }); }
        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    }
    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {
        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
    // The user explicitly opts into one context-verified choice with --fix.
    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }
}
    // ----- reads -----

    fn read(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if read.matching.is_some() && self.binding(&read.table).is_some_and(|b| matches!(b.ty.required(), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Enum(_))) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, format!("`{}` is text; `matching` searches a table's word index", read.table),
                format!("for text compare `{0} = q` or `{0} != q`; for whole-token membership use `q in tokens({0})`; for word-prefix row search write `<table> matching q first 50 by .id`", read.table)).guide("types"));
        }
        if let Some(timeline) = self.schema.timelines.iter().find(|timeline| timeline.name == read.table) {
            let expanded = if let Some(plan) = self.catalog.feed_views.get(&read.table) {
                super::feed_view::read(timeline, plan, read)?
            } else { super::timeline::read(timeline, read)? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.013#14.4 — the scale certificate trusts only the scan this expansion generates.
            let outer = self.expanding_feed.replace((timeline.table("Feed"), read.site.clone()));
            let bound = self.expr(&expanded, out);
            self.expanding_feed = outer;
            return bound;
        }
        if let Some(shaped) = self.page_shape(read) { return self.expr(&shaped, out); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — a bound row of this table addresses itself, as in `update t[row]`.
        if let Some(by_id) = self.row_as_key(read) { return self.read(&by_id, out); }
        if self.is_page(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint)? {
            let (scan, take) = self.scan(read, out)?;
            return Ok((Expr::Scan(scan), Ty::Page(read.table.clone(), take)));
        }
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            if read.keys.len() != maintain.by.len() || read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.as_ref()).any(|column| !maintain.by.contains(column)) {
                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&read.site, format!("{} scalar lookup needs the complete group key: {}", read.table, maintain.by.join(", ")), format!("supply every group key, or page the view: {} first 20 by .id after c", read.table)));
            }
        }
        let value = self.read_value(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint, out)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            // A maintained point read is the typed value: zero when absent.
            let row = self.temp("countRow");
            let tmp = self.temp("total");
            let zero = Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0));
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: row.clone(), value });
            out.push(Stmt::If {
                condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::var(&row)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) },
                then_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: zero }],
                else_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: ir::field(ir::var(&row), "value") }],
            });
            let ty = info.columns.get("value").map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Int);
            let ty = self.maintained_result_type(maintain, read, ty);
            return Ok((ir::var(&tmp), ty));
        }
        Ok((value, Ty::Row(read.table.clone()).optional()))
    }

    fn scan(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        self.scan_at(read, out, prefix::Position::Page)
    }

    fn scan_at(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body, position: prefix::Position) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        let (keys, index, selection) = self.select_index(info, read, ReadMode::Range, out)?;
        let prefix = self.order_keys(info, &index, keys)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5.1 — one pinned parent authorizes all of its aggregate groups.
        if let Some(m) = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).filter(|m| m.groups_by_parent(self.schema)) {
            let parent = self.schema.tables[&m.source].through.as_ref().unwrap();
            if m.parent_key.is_none() || !index.columns.iter().take(prefix.len()).any(|f| f == &parent.reference) {
                let fields = m.by.iter().filter(|f| *f != &parent.reference).map(|f| format!(".{f}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                let order = if fields.is_empty() { ".id" } else { &fields };
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_READ_THROUGH_SCAN",
                    format!("{} must pin {} to one readable {} row before enumerating groups", read.table, parent.reference, parent.parent),
                    format!("inside a page of {}, read `{}[{} = .id] by {order}` in its shape: `{{ *, totals: {}[{} = .id] by {order} }}`; keep the parent page's audience key, order and cursor. For a scalar supply every group key: {}[{}]. Omit an explicit aggregate read rule to inherit parent authorization", parent.parent, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, m.by.join(", "))).guide("maintains"));
            }
        }
        let (range, bound) = self.prefix_bound(read, &index, position)?;
        if info.maintained.is_some() && let Some(position) = index.columns.iter().position(|field| field == "value") {
            if prefix.len() != position {
                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&range.site, format!("{} by {} needs every ranking scope field pinned: {}", read.table, index.name, index.columns[..position].join(", ")),
                    format!("pin {} with where equalities, then read {} last {} by .value after c; omit the scope predicate for a global ranking", index.columns[..position].join(", "), read.table, range.take)));
            }
        }
        if range.take == 0 {
            return Err(type_error(&range.site, "a page holds at least one row", print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { range: Some(ast::RangeSpec { take: 50, ..range.clone() }), ..read.clone() })));
        }
        let mut page_source = read.clone();
        page_source.computed.extend(index.computed.iter().map(|(key, value)| {
            let mut value = value.clone();
            if let A::Call { args, .. } = &mut value
                && let Some(A::Name { name, site }) = args.first().cloned() {
                args[0] = A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name, site };
            }
            (key.clone(), value)
        }));
        for (key, column) in page_source.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) {
            key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone());
        }
        let filter_text = read.filter.as_ref().map(|expr| self.catalog.source.expression(expr));
        let (read, filter) = self.scan_filter(read, &index)?;
        let read = &read;
        // Key expressions precede the seek bounds and cursor, including their setup.
        let prefix: Vec<Expr> = prefix.into_iter().map(|value| self.snapshot(value, out)).collect();
        let seek = self.seek(info, read, &index, out)?;
        let mut after = None;
        if let Some(cursor) = &range.cursor {
            let (e, t) = self.expr(cursor, out)?;
            if !matches!(t.required(), Ty::Cursor) {
                return Err(type_error(cursor.site(), format!("after expects a cursor, got {}", t.name()), "declare c: cursor ? and pass after c"));
            }
            if !info.total_cursor(&index) {
                let columns = index.columns.iter().map(|column| index.computed.get(column)
                    .map(print::expression).unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                if index.unique {
                    // Do not add id to the unique constraint: that would
                    // weaken it. Plan a separate pageable index from the read.
                    let mut planned = read.clone();
                    let rewrite = if index.computed.is_empty() {
                        planned.index = None;
                        for (key, column) in planned.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) { key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone()); }
                        planned.order_keys = index.columns.iter().skip(prefix.len()).cloned().collect();
                        print::read_expr(&planned)
                    } else {
                        let mut name = format!("{}Page", index.name);
                        while info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.name == name) { name.push('_'); }
                        planned.index = Some(name.clone());
                        format!("add `by {name}: {columns}, id` to {}; read `{}`", read.table, print::read_expr(&planned))
                    };
                    return Err(Diagnostic::new(&range.site, "E_CURSOR_INDEX", format!("index {}.{} cannot carry a cursor: its nullable unique key allows tied null rows without an id tiebreak", read.table, index.name), rewrite).guide("indexes"));
                }
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&range.site, "E_CURSOR_INDEX", format!("index {}.{} cannot carry a cursor: it has no declared id or total unique key", read.table, index.name), format!("by {}: {columns}, id", index.name)).guide("indexes").mechanical());
            }
            after = Some(Box::new(e));
        }
        let mut prefix: Vec<IndexKeyExpr> = prefix.into_iter().map(IndexKeyExpr::Scalar).collect();
        if let Some(matching) = &read.matching {
            // The search text follows the keys as the word term; the index
            // was chosen for its words component in exactly that position.
            let (e, t) = self.expr(matching, out)?;
            if !matches!(t, Ty::Text(_)) {
                let rewrite = if matches!(t, Ty::Optional(ref inner) if matches!(**inner, Ty::Text(_))) { format!("{} ?? ''", print::expression(matching)) } else { "matching q  -- with q: text <=100".to_owned() };
                return Err(type_error(matching.site(), format!("matching takes present text, got {}", t.name()), rewrite));
            }
            prefix.push(IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(e));
        }
        let scan_site = self.site();
        self.page_sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), page_source);
        let pins = read.keys.iter().enumerate().map(|(position, key)| {
            let column = key.column.as_ref().unwrap_or(&index.columns[position]);
            format!(".{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
        let domain = if pins.is_empty() { read.table.clone() } else { format!("{} where {pins}", read.table) };
        self.sources.insert(format!("domain/{scan_site}"), (read.site.clone(), domain));
        if filter.is_some() && let Some(text) = filter_text { self.filters.insert(scan_site.clone(), text); }
        // Only the generated scan itself, never an authored scan bound inside its keys, cursor or helpers.
        if self.expanding_feed.as_ref().is_some_and(|(feed, site)| *feed == read.table && *site == read.site) { self.timeline_scans.insert(scan_site.clone()); }
        self.record_selection(&scan_site, read, &index, if position == prefix::Position::Exists { "exists" } else { "page" }, &selection);
        // Authored spelling serves diagnostics; inferred-index residency sites
        // keep canonical bytes. Explicit-index search alternatives retain their
        // authored origin even when presence planning synthesizes a browse index.
        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() && let Some(text) = self.catalog.canonical_read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()) {
            self.canonical_sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), text.clone());
        }
        let spelling = if !read.order_keys.is_empty() { self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned() } else { None };
        self.sources.insert(scan_site.clone(), (read.site.clone(), spelling.unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), ..read.clone() }))));
        let mut scan = Scan { cursor_pins: read.cursor_prefix.map_or(0, |old| (read.keys.len() - old) as u32),
            variant: self.variant(),
            site: scan_site,
            table: read.table.clone(),
            index: index.name,
            prefix,
                    (_, Ty::Money(c, a), Ty::Money(d, b)) if a == b && c == d && op != "*" => Ty::Money(c.clone(), *a),
                    ("*", Ty::Decimal(a), Ty::Int) | ("*", Ty::Int, Ty::Decimal(a)) => Ty::Decimal(*a),
                    ("*", Ty::Money(c, a), Ty::Int) | ("*", Ty::Int, Ty::Money(c, a)) => Ty::Money(c.clone(), *a),
                    (_, Ty::Int, Ty::Int) => Ty::Int,
                    _ => return Err(type_error(site, format!("{op} needs matching numeric types; got {} and {}", lt.name(), rt.name()), "convert one operand")),
                };
                let ir_op = match op {
                    "+" => BinaryOp::Add,
                    "-" => BinaryOp::Sub,
                    _ => BinaryOp::Mul,
                };
                return Ok((Expr::Binary { op: ir_op, left: Box::new(l), right: Box::new(r) }, result));
            }
            other => return Err(unknown(site, format!("unknown operator {other}"), "use =, !=, <, <=, >, >=, +, -, *, and, or, ??")),
        };
        let mut present = Vec::new();
        if matches!(ir_op, BinaryOp::Lt | BinaryOp::Lte | BinaryOp::Gt | BinaryOp::Gte) {
            if nullable_left { present.push(l.clone()); }
            if nullable_right { present.push(r.clone()); }
        }
        let mut comparison = Expr::Binary { op: ir_op, left: Box::new(l), right: Box::new(r) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.1 — predicate nulls do not enter ordering;
        // the ordinary interpreter's total value ordering remains unchanged.
        for value in present.into_iter().rev() {
            comparison = Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::And,
                left: Box::new(Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Ne, left: Box::new(value), right: Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)) }),
                right: Box::new(comparison) };
        }
        Ok((comparison, Ty::Bool))
    }

    /// An expression in an identity position: a text literal becomes an id.
    pub(super) fn expr_as_identity(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } = expr {
            if text.is_empty() {
                return Err(type_error(expr.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), expected.required()));
        }
        self.expr(expr, out)
    }

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
    }

- **2026-09-13 — sessions are short, rotated credentials (security review).**
  Password hashing runs outside the engine mutex behind a bounded work queue;
  password bodies and attempts per peer and normalized email are bounded, and
  unknown accounts pay the same verification work. Ordinary sessions last
  seven days. Refresh replaces the presented session, logout-all revokes every
  session for its principal, and expired sessions are pruned. Change polls and
  effect claims reauthenticate after waiting before observing or claiming new
  work. The web client
  keeps credentials in memory by default, exposes localStorage only as an
  explicit compatibility store, and refreshes a near-expiry session before it
  becomes unusable.

- **2026-09-13 — hidden rows stay hidden at write and replay boundaries
  (security review).** A unique conflict with a row the caller cannot read is
  the generic rule refusal. Audience insertion is admitted only when the
  caller already has the matching grant; audience keys cannot be changed to
  acquire one. The private mutation log may retain old operator data, but sync
  catch-up re-evaluates the current row and emits only its current readable
  image, or a deletion for a row that disappeared or expired. Previously
  delivered device data remains a property of that device.

- **2026-10-12 — a third asset kind, `file`, under the same contract
  (author, approving LLP 3000.038).** A document column (`file <=N`, with an
  optional allow-list of media types Snapback can verify) keeps one home,
  serving by the home row's read rule and the release/sweep lifetime above.
  The upload declares its type and filename (`?as=file&name=`); the server
  checks the declaration against the bytes and never stores a script-bearing
  type. A file is only ever served as an attachment (`nosniff`, a
  sandboxing CSP); `once` does not apply; nothing is transcoded.

- **2026-09-13 — assets are staged, bounded and viewer-private (security
  review).** Server IDs use operating-system or Web Crypto entropy. Upload
  bytes and asset deletion run outside the engine mutex, with bounded slots
  and a generation/session check before commit. A once asset is consumable by
  its placed recipient, while its unplaced uploader retains preview access;
  ranged reads cannot bypass consumption. Image dimensions, total pixels and
  video duration are capped. Unsatisfiable ranges return 416 and every asset
  response is `private, no-store`, so a browser cache cannot cross principals.

- **2026-09-13 — optimize the production server for speed.**
  The production Cargo profile uses optimization level 3 after an
  identical-source comparison with the former size setting. On the recorded
  100,000-row catalog, the speed build has lower median p50 in eight of eight
  HTTP cases and lower p95 in seven; continuation-page p95 worsens. The Linux
  ARM64 executable grows from 3,296,936 B to 5,787,152 B, below the recorded
scale), reads outside the partition on a device (D), and anything that
leaves the engine's write path. The rest — composition, accumulation,
filtering, seeking, grouping, formatting, defaults, patching — is §5.

## 4. Principles

1. **One language, one interpreter, every host.** Every form has one
   semantics, evaluated by one interpreter, on the server, in the wasm and
   in the native module. Where a form cannot be *evaluated* on a host — a
   fact only the server holds, a pass that only a non-predicting mutation
   may make (§5.9) — that is a residency class of the operation, printed
   by `check`, never a second syntax.
2. **The meter is the bound.** A form is admitted if the runtime meter
   bounds its cost; no static byte, step or closure price is reintroduced
   (LLP 2000.000 §6). A loop the meter cannot bound stays refused.
3. **The engine never asks for a fact it already holds.** The audience is
   the read rule; the write rule decides writes; a default the schema
   states is not restated at every insert.
4. **Residency is a fact, not a mode.** Where an operation runs and
   whether a mutation predicts are computed from the tables it reads and
   printed by `check`, carried in `api.ts`, explained by `why`.
5. **No silent class change.** A change that moves an operation from
   device to server, or a mutation from predictable to queued-without-a-
   row, is a note at `check`, never a runtime surprise.
6. **Nothing leaves the engine's write path.** Every relaxation is a read
   or a computation; writes keep rules, constraints, maintains, the log
   and at-most-once (LLP 3000.005's rule 2).
7. **Disclosure is decided once.** A write discloses nothing but its
   outcome; a read discloses rows the read rule admits; no relaxation
   adds a third channel (the blind-review territory of LLP 3000 decision
   4).
8. **The card grows one word at most**, and only by the author's ruling
   (LLP 2000.000 §8).

## 5. The language, re-derived

### 5.0 The meter charges what the new forms allocate (prerequisite)

The runtime meter is the bound this document leans on, and today it does
not charge the two things accumulation does most: `Builtin::Concat`
returns a new string or list without charging its size
(`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/runner.rs:417`), and every read of a
variable clones its value (`runner.rs:323`) without charging the copy,
while `Push` and literal construction do charge (`runner.rs:159, 334,
339`). A loop that rebinds `s = s + row.body` over 8,192 rows of 4,000
characters would allocate past the 16 MiB heap ceiling with only the step
> (`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/`).
>
> **1. Semantics.** Define the meaning of a checked Quarry program: the
> parsed and type-checked surface program, before the binder lowers it to
> IR. Cover:
>
> - the value model exactly as `value.rs` states it: i53 integers with
>   refusing overflow, nominal ids, bytes, rows, arrays and maps, the one
>   total order (text by code point), the canonical JSON and the digest;
> - expressions, including short-circuit evaluation, null assertions and
>   pure helper calls with independent evaluation of each call;
> - schema, keys and key order, and point reads, scans, counts, bounded
>   reads and pages, with completeness and cursors;
> - writes, operations, jobs and their at-most-once identity;
> - principals and rules: what a principal may read, what it may write, and
>   which refusal it receives otherwise;
> - the meter: what each step charges, where a budget cuts a read, and what
>   a cut reports;
> - expiry, ephemeral tables and maintained columns, at the level of
>   observable outcomes.
>
> The operational semantics must be executable: a Lean function from
> (schema, store state, principal, program, arguments, meter budget, clock)
> to an outcome record with the fields LLP 3000.009 §11.1 lists. Those are
> data, completeness, cut sites, cursors, every meter field, tables touched,
> next expiry, refusals and their sites. The axiomatic semantics gives
> Hoare-style rules for statements and the system's invariants as theorems
> over the operational semantics. At minimum, prove:
>
> - determinism;
> - that the value order is total and agrees with the key encoding;
> - that no read discloses a row its rules hide from the principal;
> - that no write succeeds without write authority;
> - that `complete: true` is never reported for a read that omitted a
>   qualifying row.
>
> Where the language is underspecified, do not guess silently. List each
> point in an `OPEN.md` beside the Lean project, with the Rust engine's
> current behaviour.
>
> **2. Backend.** Add a Lean emitter to `snapback4-lang` beside the
> TypeScript and Rust emitters (`src/emit.rs`, `src/emit/rust.rs`). Expose
> it through `snapback4 check` (for example `--emit-lean <dir>`). For any
> app's `snapback/*.q`, it emits a Lean module that embeds the schema, the
> rules and every query, operation and job as terms of the §1 semantics. The
> emitted module must elaborate with no `sorry`. Optionally, also emit the
> bound IR as Lean terms, with an IR-level evaluator, so that a disagreement
> can be localized to the binder or the interpreter. The surface semantics
> remains the reference.
>
> **3. Differential random testing.** Build a harness (a crate, for example
> `snapback4-difftest`, plus a compiled Lean executable that reads cases and
> writes outcomes as canonical JSON) that:
>
> - generates well-typed Quarry programs, schemas with rules, store states,
>   principals, arguments, meter budgets and clocks from a seed;
> - runs each case through the Lean reference and through the Rust engine on
>   SQLite and on the device's memory store, and on Postgres when one is
>   available;
> - runs the Rust side with prefetch on and off, and with a meter cut forced
>   at every charge position;
> - compares every outcome field after the documented normalization (none
>   for data and refusals);
> - shrinks each disagreement to a minimal Quarry source file plus store
>   state, and writes it as a replayable case that names its seed.
>
> A named unsupported case never counts as a pass. Report counts of
> supported, unsupported and disagreeing cases separately.
>
> **4. Tests and scheduled runs.** Add a large corpus of differential cases:
>
> - every case in `snapback4/corpus/` that the semantics covers;
> - every guide card's Quarry;
> - the apps under `snapback4/apps/`;
> - each reproduced disagreement in LLP 3000.003 as a named regression case;
> - shrunk counterexamples as they are found.
>
> Add a fixed-seed smoke run to `scripts/test.sh` only if it fits that
> suite's 60-second budget, and leave the Lean build outside it if the build
> does not fit. Put the large randomized runs on a schedule, in the same way
> as the Postgres suite and the drift backstops. Large means at least 10^6
> cases per run across a seed range that rotates. File each new
> disagreement signature as a ticket under `issues/` without duplicating an
> open one. Do not add a blocking gate (LLP 3000.009 §11, LLP 0189).
> Document every command in `snapback4/AGENTS.md`.
>
> When Lean and Rust disagree, neither wins by default. Classify each
> disagreement as a Rust defect, a semantics defect, or an open language
> question. Fix the first two with a regression case, and bring the third to
> Charlie with both behaviours stated. Write up what was built, what is
> proved, what is only tested, and what is not yet covered, as an LLP under
> 3000 with the evidence attached.

## 4. Assessment: is this worth doing?

Charlie asked whether this verification makes sense. The answer recorded
here is that it does, in a narrower form than §3.

**Why it is worth doing.**

- **The bug classes are real here.** LLP 3000.003 reproduced every class
  this method targets:
  - integer overflow handled differently on the device;
  - text ordered by UTF-16 unit instead of code point;
  - `identity(1) + identity(2)` returning 4;
  - a null assertion running in the skipped side of `or`;
  - a JSON 1.25 decoded as zero.

  A filed ticket also records an id/text equality divergence in rules
  (`issues/20260913-snapback4-rule-equality-id-string-divergence.md`). Random
  differential testing finds these classes well. Hand-written cases mostly
  do not.
- **There is a close precedent.** AWS's Cedar is, like Quarry's rules, an
  authorization language with a Rust implementation. Its published method
  is a Lean model, proofs of the security properties, and large-scale
  differential random testing of the Rust code against the model, which
  found real defects. Tudor's prompt reads like that recipe. This record
  cites the precedent from memory and has not re-verified its details.
- **Some claims need proof.** "No read discloses a row its rules hide,"
  proven absence (LLP 3000.031) and a certifiable unread count
  (LLP 3000.020) are claims where a proof over a model means much more than
