    /// Compare wire identity before decoding against a possibly newer schema.
    // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.1 — an id binds the admitted operation and arguments.
    pub fn replay_mutation(&self, viewer: &str, id: &str, name: &str, args: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<Option<MutationOutcome>, RuntimeError> {
        crate::store::replay_bound(self.store.conn(), viewer, id, name, args)?
            .map(|receipt| MutationOutcome::from_receipt(&receipt)).transpose()
    }

    /// Run a mutation. With an `op` id the write applies at most once: a
    /// replay returns the recorded outcome. The replay check comes before
    /// anything the current generation could refuse (an op renamed, an
    /// argument narrowed), so a write that applied under an earlier schema
    /// is never re-judged: its row is on the server, and the device that
    /// queued it must learn `sent`, not `failed`.
    pub fn mutate(&mut self, name: &str, op: Option<&str>, mut context: Context) -> Result<MutationOutcome, RuntimeError> {
        context.generation = self.generation;
        let requested = context.now.unwrap_or_else(crate::now_ms);
        context.now = Some(self.advance_time_audience(requested)?);
        let wire_args = if op.is_some() { context.wire_args.take().unwrap_or_else(|| {
                serde_json::Value::Object(crate::wire::map_to_json(&context.args))
            }) } else { serde_json::Value::Null };
        if let Some(op) = op
            && let Some(outcome) = self.replay_mutation(&context.viewer, op, name, &wire_args)?
        {
            return Ok(outcome);
        }
        // An existing durable receipt owns its id even after conversion to ephemeral.
        if self.schema.operations.get(name).is_some_and(|f| f.ephemeral) {
            self.require_non_media_ephemeral(&context.viewer,op)?;
            self.schema.check_delivery(name, context.delivery_fingerprint.as_deref())?;
            return match self.mutate_ephemeral(name, context) {
                Ok(out) => Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Sent { seq: out.seq, result: out.result }, metered: out.metered, tables: out.ephemeral.tables, events: 0, replayed: false }),
                Err(error) if error.is_refusal() => Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: error.refusal(), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false }),
                Err(error) => Err(error),
            };
        }
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        if let Some(op) = op
            && let Some(outcome) = crate::store::replay_bound(tx.conn(), &context.viewer, op, name, &wire_args)?
        {
            // Applied between the check above and this transaction.
            let outcome = MutationOutcome::from_receipt(&outcome)?;
            tx.rollback()?;
            return Ok(outcome);
        }
        if let Some(op)=op{assets::require_placement(&tx,&context.viewer,op,name,&wire_args)?;}
        context.now = Some(time_audience::pin_writer_time(
            &self.schema,
            &tx,
            context.now.unwrap_or(0),
        )?);
        self.schema.check_delivery(name, context.delivery_fingerprint.as_deref())?;
        let program = self.programs.get(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::UnknownOp(name.to_owned()))?.clone();
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is a query, not a mutation")));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is an action: call it with client.act")));
        }
        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job {
            return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownOp(format!("{name} is a job: a schedule fires it, or `snapback4 run {name}` does; no caller can")));
        }
        if let Err(error) = validate::validate_inputs(&self.schema, &program, &mut context.args) {
            return Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: error.refusal(), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false });
        }
        if context.presence.is_none() {
            context.presence = self.presence.clone();
        }
        context.entropy = self.entropy;
        let ceilings = self.ceilings;
        let viewer = context.viewer.clone();
        let now = context.now.unwrap_or(0);
        // An `image`/`video` argument names an upload by id; the program
        // reads the facts the server established (`clip.duration`), so
        // they are put in place before it runs. An id that is not the
        // writer's own upload stays as sent and the write refuses it.
        for (name, kind) in &program.args {
            let inner = match kind {
                crate::ir::ArgType::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref(),
                other => other,
            };
            if !matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::Image { .. } | crate::ir::ArgType::Video { .. } | crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) {
                continue;
            }
            let Some(id) = asset_id(context.args.get(name)).map(str::to_owned) else { continue;
            };
            if let Some(asset) = tx.asset(&id)?
                && (asset.owner == viewer || context.is_system())
            {
                // @ref LLP 3000.038#3 — a file argument never reads an image's facts, nor an image argument a file's.
                if (asset.kind == AssetKind::File) != matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) {
                    let wanted = if matches!(inner, crate::ir::ArgType::File { .. }) { "a file" } else { "an image or video" };
                    let error = RuntimeError::Asset { code: "E_ASSET_TYPE", message: format!("{name} takes {wanted} and {id} is {}; upload it as {wanted}", asset.kind.word()) };
                    return Ok(MutationOutcome { receipt_only: false, state: WriteState::Failed { why: error.refusal(), current: None }, metered: Metered::default(), tables: Vec::new(), events: 0, replayed: false });
                }
                context.args.insert(name.clone(), asset.column_value());
            }
        }
        tx.savepoint("program")?;
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, context, Meter::new(ceilings, Cut::Abort), tx.next_seq()?);
        let ran = runner.run(&program).and_then(|value| {
            runner.check_final_exists()?;
            runner.charge_output(&value)?;
            Ok(value)
        });
        let metered = runner.meter.used;
        let ran = ran.and_then(|value| runner.retire_expired_births().map(|_| value));
        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
        let events: Vec<Event> = runner.events.take();
        let action_tables = std::mem::take(&mut runner.action_tables);
        let outbox = std::mem::take(&mut runner.outbox);
        let rows_written = runner.rows_written;
        let mut read_tables = runner.read_tables.clone();
        self.entropy = runner.context.entropy;
        drop(runner);
        let outcome = match ran {
            Ok(result) => {
                tx.release("program")?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.019#4 — P7 reports keep at-most-once custody.
                // @ref LLP 3000.022#6 — a covered report keeps only a receipt at the committed head.
                let receipt_only = rows_written == 0 && outbox.is_empty()
                    && self.schema.delivery.iter().any(|binding| binding.mutation == name);
                let seq = tx.next_seq()? - u64::from(receipt_only);
                read_tables.extend(tables.iter().cloned());
                if !receipt_only {
                    tx.record_commit(seq, now, name, &viewer, program.kind.word(), &tables, rows_written)?;
                    partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
                    effects::record(&tx, &outbox, seq, now, self.dev_effects, &self.schema)?;
                }
                MutationOutcome { receipt_only, state: WriteState::Sent { seq, result }, metered, tables, events: partition::disclosed_event_count(&self.schema, &events, &action_tables), replayed: false }
            }
            Err(error) if error.is_refusal() => {
                tx.rollback_to("program")?;
                let current = match &error {
                    RuntimeError::Conflict { current } => {
                        let mut probe = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::new(viewer.clone(), BTreeMap::new()).at(now), Meter::new(Ceilings::default(), Cut::Cap), 0);
                        let value = probe.get(current).ok();
                        value
                    }
                    _ => None,
                };

                // context for denial, hidden collision and authorization exhaustion.

                }

            }
            Err(error) => {
                tx.rollback()?;
                return Err(error);
            }
        };

            let seq = match &outcome.state {
                WriteState::Sent { seq, .. } => *seq,
                WriteState::Failed { .. } => tx.next_seq()? - 1,
//! A request scope plus a response-layer backstop, never a caller channel.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — missing instrumentation must supersede older evidence.
use super::{Recent, Sites};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::{cell::RefCell, sync::{Arc, atomic::Ordering}};

thread_local! { static CURRENT: RefCell<Option<Evidence>> = const { RefCell::new(None) }; }
struct Evidence {
    recent: Recent,
    operation: String,
    request: u64,
    query: bool,
    trace: RefusalTrace,
    sites: Arc<Sites>,
    generation: Option<u64>,
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct RequestScope;
impl Drop for RequestScope { fn drop(&mut self) { CURRENT.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take()); } }
pub(in crate::serve) fn begin(shared: &crate::serve::Shared, path: &str) -> Option<RequestScope> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let recent = shared.refusals.clone();
    let request = recent.requests.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
    CURRENT.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(Evidence { recent,
        operation: path.strip_prefix("/q/").or_else(|| path.strip_prefix("/m/")).unwrap_or(path).into(),
        request, query: path.starts_with("/q/"), trace: Default::default(), sites: Default::default(), generation: None }));
    Some(RequestScope)
}
pub(in crate::serve) fn context(context: Context, sites: Arc<Sites>, generation: u64) -> Context {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let Some(evidence) = c.as_mut() else { return context };
        evidence.sites = sites;
        evidence.generation = Some(generation);
        context.with_refusal_trace(evidence.trace.clone())
    })
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct Pending { recent: Recent, record: Value }
impl Drop for Pending {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.recent.submit(std::mem::take(&mut self.record)); }
}
// Inspect the actual encoded envelope, after overflow substitution. This covers
// every responder using the shared JSON response layer, including receipt replay,
// static admission and future paths with no attached collector. No user data is
// copied into the record. Unknown empty answers enter only the opt-in local
// trace; successful visibility filtering is never a refusal.
pub(in crate::serve) fn response(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Pending> {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let evidence = c.as_mut()?;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
        if evidence.query && evidence.trace.hidden() > 0 {
            eprintln!("dev query: {} hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", evidence.operation, evidence.trace.hidden());
        }
        let code = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why")).and_then(|v| v.get("code")).and_then(Value::as_str);
        let traced = evidence.trace.take();
        let why = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why"));
        // The one filtered read that is evidence about a program refusal: the
        // refusing `t[x] ! else CODE` lookup itself, whose read rule hid the row.
        let hidden = traced.as_ref().filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule").and_then(|cause| {
            let site = why?.get("site")?.as_str()?;
            evidence.sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))).then(|| (cause.table.clone(), evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into())).cloned()))
        });
        let cause = traced.filter(|cause| {
            // A filtered read earlier in a program is not evidence about a
            // later uninstrumented terminal refusal in that same request.
            code.is_none() || cause.terminal
        });
        let empty_answer = code.is_none();
        let code = match code {
            Some(code) => code,
            None if evidence.query && body.get("data").is_some() && body.get("loading") != Some(&Value::Bool(true)) => {
                // Visibility filtering is a successful read, not a refusal.
                if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| !cause.terminal && !cause.no_candidate) { return None; }
                if evidence.recent.trace_empty && empty(&body["data"]) { "EMPTY_READ" }
                else { return None; }
            }
            None => return None,
        };
        let preflight = code == "E_AUTH" && evidence.generation.is_none() && why.and_then(|v| v.get("site")).is_none();
        let mut record = json!({"preflight":preflight,"operation":evidence.operation, "code":code, "requestId":evidence.request,
            "generation":evidence.generation, "clause":"cause not instrumented", "at":snapback4_core::now_ms()});
        // A program's own `require … else CODE` (or `! else CODE`) carries
        // its source site in the refusal itself: the response's own
        // evidence, not an older trace, so the log can name the line.
        if cause.is_none() && let Some(why) = why
            && why.get("rule").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some(code) && let Some(site) = why.get("site").and_then(Value::as_str) {
            record["clause"] = json!("program refusal");
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["siteKind"] = json!("program");
            if let Some((table, rule)) = &hidden {
                record["hiddenBy"] = json!({"table": table, "readRuleSite": rule});
            }
        }
        if empty_answer {
            record["emptyRead"] = json!(true);
            record["cause"] = json!(if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| cause.no_candidate) { "no-candidate" } else { "unrecorded" });
            record["clause"] = json!("no probe miss and no rule cause were recorded");
        }
        if let Some(cause) = cause {
            let clause = evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.phase.clone()))
                .or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.clause.clone())));
            let site = clause.or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "table".into())));
            // Whether the site is the clause's own line or the table's (an omitted rule).
            record["siteKind"] = json!(if clause.is_some() { "clause" } else if site.is_some() { "table" } else { "" });
            record["table"] = json!(cause.table);
            record["clause"] = json!(cause.clause);
            record["phase"] = json!(cause.phase);
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["executionSite"] = json!(cause.site);
            record["generation"] = json!(cause.generation);
            record["readRuleSite"] = json!(evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table, "read rule".into())));
        }
        // Published before sending: why refuses an old disk snapshot even when
        // the writer is blocked, this handoff drops, or responses finish out of order.
        Some(Pending { recent: evidence.recent.clone(), record: evidence.recent.sequence(record) })
    })
}
fn empty(value: &Value) -> bool {
    match value {
        Value::Null => true,
        Value::Array(values) => values.is_empty(),
        _ => false,
    }
}
//! A row-read verdict and field projection together authorize result pricing.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, ir::Read, meter::Meter,
    schema::{Effect, Index}, value::{Disclosed, Value}};

// Only successful row authorization in this module can mint this evidence.
pub(crate) struct RowAuthorization(());
pub(crate) struct AuthorizedRow<'a> {
    projection: Disclosed<'a>,
    authority: RowAuthorization,
    // @ref LLP 3000.014#5 — a row's witness deadline is provisional until an answer emits it.
    expiry: super::expiry::Pending,
}
impl AuthorizedRow<'_> {
    pub(super) fn expiry(&self) -> super::expiry::Pending { self.expiry }
    pub(super) fn scalar_heap(&self, field: &str) -> u64 { self.projection.scalar_heap(field, &self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn heap_bytes(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.heap_bytes(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn projection_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.projection_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(crate) fn materialization_heap(&self) -> u64 { self.projection.materialization_heap(&self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn into_value(self) -> Value { self.projection.into_value() }
    pub(super) fn into_parts(self) -> (Value, RowAuthorization) { (self.projection.into_value(), self.authority) }
    pub(super) fn charge(self, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        meter.heap(self.heap_bytes() + self.projection_heap() + self.materialization_heap())?;
        Ok(self.into_value())
    }
}

impl RowAuthorization {
    pub(super) fn project(self, projection: Disclosed<'_>) -> AuthorizedRow<'_> { AuthorizedRow { projection, authority:self, expiry: Default::default() } }
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — member reads share the row-read and field-disclosure capability.
    pub(super) fn authorized_member(&mut self, name: &str, id: &str, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] trusted: bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.024#decision — this id came from an authorized head in
        // this read-only transaction. Price its public image before decoding,
        // then recheck the rule with a separate witness reservation. Neither
        // hidden/expired images nor private fields can select actual-size admission.
        #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
        if trusted && !self.device && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive() && table.recorded.is_empty()
            && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a|a.audience) {
            if !self.read_charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)? { return Ok(None); }
            self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::text(id) + crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<Value>(1))?;
            let key = [Value::Id(id.into())];
            let Some((Some(row),_)) = self.scan_authorized(name,crate::schema::BY_ID,&key,&Default::default(),crate::rows::Dir::Asc,1,true,true,None)?.pop() else { return Ok(None); };
            let materialization = row.materialization_heap();
            let prepay = self.meter.mark();
            self.meter.heap(materialization)?;
            let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
            let row = self.authorized_scratch(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read), &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner,_| {
                let row = runner.authorize_image(name,row.into_value())?;
                if let Some(row) = &row { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); }
                Ok(row)
            });
            // The value now owns the default. Transfer its prepay to charge(),
            // keeping only one retained reservation after the recheck.
            self.meter.release_row_heap(prepay, materialization);
            let row = row?;
            if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
            return row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose();
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        let row = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::Examined, 1), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read),
                )], |runner, scope| {
            let Some(mut row) = runner.tx.get(scope, name, id)? else { return Ok(None); };
            crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
            let until = table.deadline(&row);
            let authorized = runner.authorize_image(name, row)?;
            if let Some(row) = &authorized { runner.next_expiry.inherit(row.expiry()); runner.next_expiry.note_option(until); }
            Ok(authorized)
        })?;
        if row.is_some() { expiry.emit(); }
        row.map(|row|row.charge(&mut self.meter)).transpose()
    }

    pub(super) fn authorize_image(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#5-what-derives-from-the-capture — a denied or expired candidate's witness deadlines are discarded with its scope.
        let expiry = self.next_expiry.scope();
        if self.inactive(name, &row)? { return Ok(None); }
        if !self.check_rule(name, Effect::Read, None, &row)? {
            self.hidden_row();
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Memory deadlines are hidden by Disclosed itself; transfer the live
        // boundary before projecting, still inside this candidate's expiry scope.
        if table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.note_expiry(table, &row); }
        let projection = self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query();
        let visible = projection.into_value();
        Ok(Some(AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, visible).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: expiry.defer() }))
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_disclosed(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized(name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,true,true,#[cfg(feature = "server-search")] None)
    }

    pub(super) fn scan_authorized(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.scan_authorized_prepaid(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered, trusted, false, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields)
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.7 — a delivered uniform prefix has prepaid its
    // logical rule once; provisional rows still undergo physical authorization.
    pub(super) fn scan_authorized_prepaid(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool, #[cfg(feature = "server-search")] fields: Option<&[String]>) -> Result<Vec<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        // A public, non-expiring image is authorized before accessing storage.
        // No hidden candidate can influence its actual-size decoder admission.
        // Trusted selected heads may use the same actual-size adapter. It
        // decodes a full transient image; the streaming consumer retains only
        // its ordering key. Selection remains useful in the private decoder.
        if (self.context.is_system() || trusted && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive()) && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a| a.audience) {
            if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>,Vec<u8>,
                )>(limit))?; }
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            let live = table.read_lifetime_sensitive();
            // A web device never reads as the system, the only reader of an expiring table here.
            #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
            let live = false;
            let rows = if live { self.live_store_scan(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered)? }
            else if metered {
                self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,&mut runner.meter)
                })?
            } else { self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit)
                })? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.5 — a device prices the server's image with the server's charge. Its
            // own prediction rows still meet a non-uniform rule, as prepaid internal work outside the caller's

exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '540,660p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs; sed -n '1110,1180p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs; rg -n 'expr_as_identity|assignable_to\\(.*expected|ArgType' snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program | head -60; sed -n '1,120p' llp/3000.035-quarry-semantics-in-lean.plan.md; sed -n '365,410p' llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag
 succeeded in 0ms:
        Ok((scan, range.take))
    }

    /// Evaluate the keys, then choose the index: explicit `by`, else by
    /// key names, count and types. Point reads need a unique full key;
    /// ranges need a strict prefix.
    fn select_index(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, ast::IndexDecl, String)> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — retain bound keys for type selection and final lowering.
        // No cloned expression pass: nested lookups must not multiply binder work.
        let mut keys = Vec::<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>::new();
        let mixed = read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) && read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none());
        let types = if mixed {
            let prefix = read.keys.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
            self.bind_key_parts(info, read, prefix, &mut keys, out)?;
            Some(keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>())
        } else { None };
        let mut normalized = super::mixed_keys::read_typed(info, read, self.catalog, types.as_deref())?;
        self.name_bound_keys(info, &normalized, &mut keys)?;
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        super::validate_key_names(read)?;
        let mut positional_reason = None;
        if !read.keys.is_empty() && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
            let declared = |index: &&ast::IndexDecl| !self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()));
            let mut candidates = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| match &read.index {
                Some(name) => &index.name == name,
                None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len(),
            }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 {
                self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
                let types = keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                candidates = super::positional_keys::candidates(info, read, &types, &self.catalog.synthesized);
            }
            if read.index.is_none() && mode == ReadMode::Range && read.order_keys.is_empty() && candidates.len() == 1 {
                let scan = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).find(|index| !index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() > read.keys.len());
                let columns = scan.map(|index| index.columns.as_slice()).unwrap_or(&candidates[0].columns);
                let predicate = read.keys.iter().zip(columns).map(|(key, column)| format!(".{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", "a positional key selects one declared unique row, not a scan prefix",
                    format!("{} where {predicate} first 20 by .id", read.table)).guide("reads"));
            }
            if candidates.len() != 1 {
                return Err(super::positional_keys::refusal(info, read, &candidates, self.catalog));
            }
            if read.index.is_none() {
                positional_reason = Some(if info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).count() == 1 {
                    "only declared unique key of this arity"
                } else { "unique positional key selected by argument types" });
                normalized.to_mut().index = Some(candidates[0].name.clone());
            }
        }
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        let point = mode != ReadMode::Range;
        self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
        let named = keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| c.is_some()) && !keys.is_empty();
        let mut selection = if read.index.is_some() { "explicit by" } else if named { "named equality columns" } else { "compatible positional key types" };
        // A `words(..)` index serves one read: `matching` with exactly its
        // leading columns as keys. Every other read sees plain indexes only.
        let searching = mode == ReadMode::Range && read.matching.is_some();
        let mut candidates: Vec<&ast::IndexDecl> = info
            .indexes
            .iter()
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|explicit| explicit == &index.name))
            .filter(|index| read.seeks.iter().all(|b| index.columns.get(keys.len()) == Some(&b.column)))
            .filter(|index| if searching { index.searches_after(keys.len()) } else { index.words.is_none() })
            .filter(|index| match mode {
                ReadMode::Point | ReadMode::FilteredPoint => index.unique && (index.columns.len() == keys.len() || (named && mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() < keys.len())),
                ReadMode::Exists => (index.columns.len() == keys.len() && (named || read.index.is_some() || index.unique)) || (named && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() < keys.len()),
                ReadMode::Range => index.columns.len() > keys.len() || (!read.order_keys.is_empty() && index.unique && index.columns.len() == keys.len()),
            })
            .filter(|index| {
                if named {
                    if index.columns.len() < keys.len() { index.columns.iter().all(|column| keys.iter().any(|(c, _, _)| c.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| index.columns[..keys.len()].contains(c.as_ref().unwrap())) }
                } else {
                    true
                }
            })
            .collect();
        if named && matches!(mode, ReadMode::FilteredPoint | ReadMode::Exists) {
            if let Some(width) = candidates.iter().filter(|i| info.total_unique(i)).map(|i| i.columns.len()).min() {
                candidates.retain(|i| info.total_unique(i) && i.columns.len() == width);
                // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — named equalities covering two total unique keys
                // (`loans[borrower = viewer, telescope = t]`, lane 057) test one row either
                // way, the other key's columns being extra equalities on it; the first
                // declared key decides, as a rule probe already does (§11).
                // Same-column keys in two orders (`unique pair: low, high` and `unique
                // inverse: high, low`) still need an explicit `by` (guide indexes).
                let mut sets = candidates.iter().map(|index| { let mut columns = index.columns.clone(); columns.sort(); columns }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                sets.dedup();
                if candidates.len() > 1 && sets.len() == candidates.len() {
                    selection = "first declared unique key the named equalities cover";
                    candidates.truncate(1);
                }
            } else if candidates.iter().any(|i| i.unique) {
                // Exact nullable unique keys still select points. A competing
                // nonunique index must not turn an exists probe into a tie.
                candidates.retain(|i| i.unique);
            }
        }
        // Positional keys select by type; a text literal may meet an
        // identity column (it becomes an id once the index is chosen).
        if !named {
            candidates.retain(|index| {
                keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, (_, e, t))| {
                    index.columns.get(position).is_some_and(|column| {
                        let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, column);
                        t.comparable(&expected) || (expected.is_identity() && matches!(e, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))
                    })
                })
            });
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.5 — resolve remaining ambiguity without replacing an existing exact-type winner.
            let exact: Vec<_> = candidates.iter().copied().filter(|index| keys.iter().enumerate().all(|(position, (_, _, t))| index.columns.get(position).is_some_and(|column| self.key_ty(info, index, column) == t.required()))).collect();
            if exact.len() == 1 {
                candidates = exact;
                if read.index.is_none() { selection = "single exact positional type match"; }
            }
            else {
                // Filter before the one-candidate arm so identity literals receive their index's type.
                let unique: Vec<_> = candidates.iter().copied().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns.len() == keys.len()).collect();
                if unique.len() == 1 {
                    candidates = unique;
                    if read.index.is_none() { selection = "only compatible full unique key"; }
                }
            WriteOp::Delete => {
                let key = self.write_key(info, keys, site, out).map_err(|error| self.extra_write_test(info, expr, error))?;
                if key.alternate {
                    let location = self.site();
                    self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (site.clone(), format!("delete {table}")));
                    out.push(Stmt::DeleteTarget { target: Read { site: location, table: table.clone(), index: key.index, key: key.key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape } });
                } else {
                    out.push(Stmt::Delete { table: table.clone(), key: key.key[0].clone() });
                }
                Ok(valued.then(|| (Expr::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null)))
            }
        }
    }

    /// `t[row]` where `row` is a present row of `t`: the same read as `t[row.id]`.
    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
        by_id.keys[0].value = A::Member { base: Box::new(key.clone()), name: "id".into(), site: key.site().clone() };
        Some(by_id)
    }

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }

        }
        Ok(WriteKey { index: index.name, key, alternate: true })
    }

# LLP 3000.035: Differential testing of Quarry, then a small Lean core

**Type:** Plan
**Status:** Draft
**Systems:** Quarry (`snapback4-lang`: parser, binder, emitters), the program IR and interpreter (`snapback4-core`), the device interpreter (`snapback4-device`), the SQLite and Postgres stores, a new differential-testing crate, a later Lean 4 project, scheduled verification
**Author:** Tudor (the original prompt, §1); Claude Opus 5.5 for Charlie Cheever (the record, the Quarry adaptation, the assessment and the staged plan, §2–§6)
**Date:** 2026-10-07
**Revised:** 2026-10-13 (FLAKE: serialize cold oracle refreshes across threads/processes, §5.2); 2026-10-13 (PAGE2 pinned-clock lifetime and exact membership context pointers); 2026-10-12 (LANG2 post-merge: refused commutations compare semantics, with counted meter omissions, in §5.1); 2026-10-12 (revision 15: parent-pinned page guards in the engine and the model, in §5.2.); 2026-10-12 (revision 14: the model realigned with LLP 3000.036 §12.1, §15.4 and §15.12, in §5.2.); 2026-10-12 (lane EX integration: records the live-row meter ruling and the unproved-insert refusal fix, in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 13: records the generator widening (lane GW), the prefetch control and their findings in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 12: R2 was an engine defect, a security fix: an unnamed `update <- R` now judges old and next, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 11: records the heap fix in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 10: records the Lean emitter and oracle, its run, its negative controls and the classification of its disagreements, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 9: records the supplied-id prediction ruling in §5.1 slice C.); 2026-10-08 (revision 8: records Stage 2's model and proofs as built, in §5.2.); 2026-10-08 (revision 7: records slice C, the server against the device, in §5.1.); 2026-10-08 (revision 6: the nightly schedule is installed on expo-build-450a.); 2026-10-08 (revision 5: records Charlie's heap-accounting ruling in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 4: records the first 3-hour store run in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 3: records Stage 1 slice B (stores, prefetch, scheduled runner) as landed, with its first finding, in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 2: records Stage 1 slice A as landed, with its run and negative controls, in §5.1.); 2026-10-07 (revision 1: adds the assessment (§4) and replaces the full-scope adaptation with a two-stage plan (§5), at Charlie's direction. Stage 1 is a random program generator and Rust-against-Rust differential testing. Stage 2 is a small Lean core for the security claims. §3 is kept as the record of what was not adopted.); 2026-10-07 (draft)
**Related:** [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md), [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md) (one semantics; the reproduced disagreements), [LLP 3000.009 §11](./3000.009-general-purpose-backend.rfc.md#11-verification-obligations) (verification obligations), [LLP 3000.007](./3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md) (the current Quarry), [LLP 0189](./0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md) (cadence), [LLP 4001](./4001-snapback5-sql-front-end.rfc.md) (a second front end over the same engine)

## 0. What this records

Tudor wrote a prompt for Opus 5.5 that asks for a formal semantics of the
Contract language and a differential test of Contract's Rust interpreter
against it. Charlie asked for the prompt to be recorded and then adapted to
verify Quarry instead of Contract.

§1 is the prompt verbatim. §2 lists what has to change for Quarry, with the
reason for each change. §3 is that adaptation in full. §4 assesses whether it
is worth doing, and concludes that the full scope is not. §5 is the plan
adopted instead. §6 lists what is left open.

Nothing here has been run yet. This is a plan, not a result.

## 1. The original prompt (verbatim)

Recorded 2026-10-07 as Charlie relayed it. Contract lives
outside this repository.

> Here's the prompt for Opus 5.5
>
> Write an axiomatic and operational semantics for the Contract language.
> Then write a code generator backend for the compiler that emits an
> embedding in those semantics of any user code. Then write a differential
> random testing library that can do large-scale testing of the generated
> code (using the operational semantics) against the Rust interpreter
> version. Then add a large number of test scripts to the repo and update CI
> to do differential random testing between the lean and rust versions

The prompt has four steps, and the adaptation keeps all four:

1. axiomatic and operational semantics (in Lean, per the last sentence);
2. a compiler backend that embeds any user program in those semantics;
3. a differential random tester, Lean against Rust;
4. a large test corpus and CI that runs the differential test.

## 2. What changes for Quarry, and why

**Which program the semantics describes.** Quarry has two candidate
objects: the checked surface program the binder receives, and the IR it
emits (`snapback4-core/src/ir.rs`). The server and device interpreters
already share that IR. A semantics of the IR alone would compare one IR in
two interpreters, so the binder would never be tested. Two of the
disagreements LLP 3000.003 reproduced were binder defects:
`identity(1) + identity(2)` returned 4, and a null assertion ran in the
skipped right side of `true or …`. The adapted prompt therefore defines the
meaning of the checked surface program. An IR-level embedding is optional
and serves to localize failures. It is not the reference.

**What an outcome is.** The original prompt does not say what an outcome
is. A Quarry read or operation returns more: data, completeness, cut sites,
cursors, meter fields, the tables touched, the next expiry, and refusals
with their sites. LLP 3000.009 §11.1 already lists these fields as the
outcome every host must agree on, so the comparison uses the same list. A
differential test that compares only returned rows would miss most of what
Snapback 4 promises.

**Authority is part of the meaning.** Rules decide what a principal can read
and write, and disclosure is the property the simulator's exhaustive test
checks against a model. The semantics must include principals and rules.
That makes disclosure and write authority axioms, not test fixtures.

**The value model is specific.** Integers are i53 and overflow refuses.
There is one total order over values (text compares by code point, not by
UTF-16 unit), one canonical JSON, and one digest. Each of these produced a
reproduced cross-host bug. The Lean model must state them exactly.

**There is more than one Rust executor.** The reference is compared with the
engine over SQLite, the engine over Postgres (on a schedule, as
`scripts/test-postgres.sh` runs today), and the device interpreter over its
memory store. Prefetch on and off, and a meter cut forced at each charge
position, are part of the same comparison (§11.1).

**CI follows this repository's verification law.** The blocking suite
(`scripts/test.sh`) has a 60-second budget. LLP 3000.009 §11 says no new
blocking gate follows from these obligations, and LLP 0189 puts the complete
battery on a schedule, with reds becoming fix-forward tickets. Large-scale
random runs therefore go on a schedule. At most a small fixed-seed smoke
check joins the fast path, and only if it fits the budget. "Update CI"
becomes "schedule the large runs and file what they find."

**Disagreements need a ruling, not a winner.** When Lean and Rust disagree,
the semantics is not automatically right, because it is new and unreviewed.
Each disagreement is ticketed. The ruling (Rust bug, semantics bug, or an
underspecified point the language must decide) is recorded in the governing
LLP.

**Existing assets seed the corpus.** The repository already has the
conformance corpus (`snapback4/corpus/`, 327 JSON cases), the guide's cards
(every card's Quarry is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs`),
the apps under `snapback4/apps/`, the simulator's rules model, and Kani
proofs of the key encoding. The large test corpus starts from these.

## 3. The adapted prompt (full scope; not adopted)

This is the faithful adaptation of §1. It is kept as the record of what was
considered. §4 explains why it was not adopted, and §5 replaces it. It has
one known flaw beyond its scope. It tells the agent to read the binder and
the interpreter before writing the semantics, which invites a model that
copies the code's mistakes (§4).

> Write an axiomatic and an operational semantics for Quarry, Snapback 4's
> data language, in Lean 4. Put them in a Lake project at `snapback4/lean/`
> with a pinned `lean-toolchain`. Avoid Mathlib unless a proof needs it,
> because build time matters for the scheduled runs. Read `snapback4/AGENTS.md`,
> LLP 3000, LLP 3000.003, LLP 3000.007 and LLP 3000.009 §11 first. Then read
> the language card (`snapback4 guide language`), the binder
> (`crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/`), the IR (`crates/snapback4-core/src/ir.rs`),
> the value model (`crates/snapback4-core/src/value.rs`) and the interpreter
> (`crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/`).

| # | Today | Proposed | Source | Bound by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | A local binds once ("duplicate local") | `x = expr` rebinds a local of the same type; a `for` may carry a counter or a running sum | S | the step meter; `Stmt::Assign` exists |
| 2 | No accumulators | `xs = []` then `xs = xs + [v]` (the binder emits `Push`); `s = s + n`; a list built in a loop is bounded by the heap meter and may be iterated | S | the heap meter (`Push` charges) and the step meter |
| 3 | `continue` refused | `continue` | S (minimalism) | `Stmt::Continue` exists |
| 4 | `else if` refused | `else if cond:` as sugar for the nesting (`elif` is a guess `check --fix` rewrites, §5.5) | S (minimalism) | nothing to bound |
| 5 | `where` refused | `t[prefix] [after c] first\|last N by idx where <predicate>`: the scan proceeds in index order; a row passing the read rule and the predicate counts toward N; examined rows and probes are metered | S + A's completeness class | the examined-rows and probe meters; §5.6 fixes completeness and cursors |
| 6 | Text `+` refused | `text + text` → `Concat`; `slice`, `tokens`, `count(text)`, `lower(text)`; no `format`, no interpolation | S (bytes) and a product discipline | the output and heap meters |
| 7 | `/` and `%` refused | `a / b` (integer quotient) and `a % b` beside `divmod`; zero refuses `E_DIV_ZERO` | S (a semantic preference) | nothing to bound |

**2026-09-27 — list expressions (0.4.5 lane QE).** `a + b` concatenates
two lists in every expression position, including returns, arguments and
record fields. Item types join as in list literals: incompatible nonempty
items refuse immediately, including references to different tables and
conflicts nested inside records or lists. Empty lists supply no competing
item type. Compatible scalar items retain a common representation. Two enums
join to their deduplicated variant union, including subsets and equivalent sets
in different orders; an existing superset keeps its order, otherwise the union
follows encounter order. Quarry imposes no variant-count limit. Self-append
checks both operands against the joined item type, so disjoint enums also
keep their original string values. Enum and text widen to text bounded by both
the longest variant and the text operand,
under the existing text character-domain rule. If an enum item cannot join,
the diagnostic teaches a typed identity helper such as
`fn state(v: enum('on', 'off')): v`, with all variants, and
`[state(a), state(b)]` when every value belongs to that enum; this preserves
values without conversion. Generic id with a reference or
principal becomes id; int with decimal or money retains that numeric type
without rescaling; json accepts other known, non-row values. Unknown is never
a widening candidate in a nonempty item join. A mixed-reference refusal teaches
the intentional loss of table specificity: `fn exposedId(value: id): value`,
then `[exposedId(x), exposedId(y)]`, or `exposedId(row.id)` in each projected
field for a nested `[].id` conflict. An effect's `once [recipient, period]` is a positional key tuple,
not an iterable list, so its coordinates may differ in type. Bounds add, saturating
at the existing meter-bound sentinel. The shared `Concat` builtin charges
the result allocation. `xs = xs + [x]` retains its in-place `Push` lowering.
Other loop-carried concatenations retain the existing heap-bound widening
when their item type stabilizes; recursive item growth refuses with a
self-append rewrite using a fixed item type. A list plus a non-list names
both operand types and suggests `xs + [x]`. The recursive-growth rewrite
also covers concatenations within conditional assignments and failures caught
at reassignment, after the assigned expression typechecks; an ill-typed `+`
keeps its operand diagnostic without a recursive-growth cascade. Consuming
the accumulator in a scalar, such as `xs = xs + [count(xs)]`, also keeps the
ordinary item-join diagnostic; only embedding the accumulator in the resulting

    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
        by_id.keys[0].value = A::Member { base: Box::new(key.clone()), name: "id".into(), site: key.site().clone() };
        Some(by_id)
    }

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }
        let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: info.decl.name.clone(), keys: keys.to_vec(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
        let (bound, index, _) = self.select_index(info, &read, ReadMode::Point, out)?;
        super::point_key::check(info, &index, &read, &bound.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, bound)?;
        if index.name == "byId" {
            let id = self.temp("key");
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: key[0].clone() });
            return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
        }
        Ok(WriteKey { index: index.name, key, alternate: true })
    }

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

        let bounds = self.closure_bounds(parent)?;
        let mut price = [0_u64; 7];
        let mut heap = 0_u64;
        let mut identities = 0_u64;
        let mut gathered = 0_u64;
        let rows_of = |name: &str| bounds.iter().find(|(t, _)| t == name).map_or(0, |(_, n)| *n);
        for (table, n) in &bounds {
            let n = *n;
            if table != parent {
                // Fetched by the closure walk and again, current, before its delete.
                let unit = self.unit_write(table)?;
                heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n)));
                identities = identities.saturating_add(n);
            }
            for r in self.referencing(table) {
                // A capped walk holds one page of child images per seek.
                let row = crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling(r.table);
                heap = heap.max(row.saturating_mul(REFERENCE_PAGE as u64));
                let seeks = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && r.table.through.as_ref().is_none_or(|t| t.reference != r.column) { 2 } else { 1 };
                // A walk over a capped group: a seek per page, and a last, short page per range.
                let walk = |cap: u64| seeks * n + cap.saturating_mul(n) / REFERENCE_PAGE as u64;
                // Every reached row runs the lifetime hook for every through
                // child, including children the closure already cascaded away.
                let through = r.table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == r.column);
                if through && table != parent { add(&mut price, [3_u64, 0, 3, 0, 0, 0, 0].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(n))); }
                match r.action {
                    // A read-through child's probe: three seeks per closure row (LLP 3000.014 §6).
                    OnDelete::Restrict if through => {},
                    // One probe per closure row, a row per seek: it may skip the closure
                    // rows that name it (each names one target per reference), then stops
                    // at its first outside witness.
                    OnDelete::Restrict => {
                        let probe = seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n;
                        add(&mut price, [2 * probe, probe, seeks * n + rows_of(r.child) + n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::Cascade => {
                        let pages = walk(u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?));
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                    }
                    OnDelete::SetNull => {
                        let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
                        gathered = gathered.max(cap);
                        let cleared = cap.saturating_mul(n);
                        let unit = self.unit_write(r.child)?;
                        heap = heap.max(unit[4]);
                        let pages = walk(cap);
                        add(&mut price, [2 * pages, pages, seeks * n, 0, 0, 0, 0]);
                        add(&mut price, [unit[0], unit[1], unit[2] + 1, unit[3], 0, unit[5], unit[6]].map(|v| v.saturating_mul(cleared)));
                    }
                    OnDelete::Keep => {}
                }
            }
        }
        // The root is read again, current, before its own removal.
        price[2] = price[2].saturating_add(1);
        // The closure's identities (table and id) are held until the last
        // delete, with the root's and one page of child ids.
        // A set-null group is gathered whole (at most its cap) before it is cleared.
        price[4] = heap.saturating_add(identities.saturating_add(1 + REFERENCE_PAGE as u64 + gathered).saturating_mul(CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES));
        let mut receiver_steps = 0;
        for (table, _) in &bounds {
            for reference in self.referencing(table) {
                if reference.action.writes() { receiver_steps = receiver_steps.max(self.write_reservation(reference.child)?.action_events?.steps_per_write); }
            }
        }
        price[0] = price[0].saturating_add(price[3].saturating_mul(receiver_steps));
        price[4] = price[4].saturating_add(self.retained_action_heap(parent, price[3])?);
        Some(price)
    }

    pub fn mutate_ephemeral(
        &mut self,
        name: &str,
        mut context: Context,
    ) -> Result<Outcome, RuntimeError> {
        let program = self
            .programs
            .get(name)

        if program.kind != ProgramKind::Mutation
            || !self
                .schema

        context.minted_ids.clear();
        if context.now.is_none() {

use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}
    }
}
//! A table-typed argument is an id with a stronger name. Storing it in a
//! reference column still does not prove that the target row exists.

use super::catalog::Catalog;
use crate::{
    ast::{Expr, FunctionBody, ProgramKind, ReadExpr, Stmt, TypeKind, WriteOp},
    diag::{Diagnostic, Site},
};
use snapback4_core::schema::{Constraint, RuleExpr, RuleTerm, Schema};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Candidate {
    argument: String,
    target: String,
    child: String,
    column: String,
    op: WriteOp,
    site: Site,
    checked: bool,
}

#[derive(Clone)]
struct Flow {
    /// Arguments whose name still denotes the mutation input, rather than a
    /// later binding with the same spelling.
    inputs: BTreeSet<String>,
    checked: BTreeSet<String>,
}

/// Name a likely missing existence check. Known helpers count only when their
/// body actually looks up the argument; passing an id through does not.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — reference types validate id shape, never target
// existence; `N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED` points to the explicit lookup form.
pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for program in doc
            .programs
            .iter()
            .filter(|program| program.kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && !program.native)
        {
            let arguments: BTreeMap<String, String> = program
                .args
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|arg| match &arg.type_.kind {
                    TypeKind::Ref { table } => Some((arg.name.clone(), table.clone())),
                    _ => None,
                })
                .collect();
            if arguments.is_empty() {
                continue;
            }

            let mut candidates = Vec::new();
            let flow = Flow {
                inputs: arguments.keys().cloned().collect(),
                checked: BTreeSet::new(),
            };
            let body: Vec<_> = program
                .prelude
                .iter()
                .chain(&program.body)
                .cloned()
                .collect();
            flow_statements(catalog, schema, &arguments, &body, flow, &mut candidates);

            let mut emitted = BTreeSet::new();
            for candidate in candidates {
                if candidate.checked
                    || write_contract_checks(schema, &candidate)
                    || !emitted.insert(candidate.argument.clone())
                {
                    continue;
                }
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&candidate.site, "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED",
                    format!("mutation {} stores argument {} directly in {}.{}, but never looks up {}[{}]: a reference accepts any well-formed id even when the row is absent", program.name, candidate.argument, candidate.child, candidate.column, candidate.target, candidate.argument),
                    format!("bind `parent = {}[{}] ! else NOT_FOUND` before the write, then store `parent.id`; keep the direct id only when dangling references are intentional", candidate.target, candidate.argument)).guide("types"));
            }
        }
    }
    notes
}

/// Follow only paths that can reach a write. A lookup fact joins by
/// intersection, so one conditional arm, a short-circuited operand, or a
/// possibly-empty loop can never authorize a later candidate.
fn flow_statements(
    catalog: &Catalog,
    schema: &Schema,
    arguments: &BTreeMap<String, String>,
    body: &[Stmt],
    mut flow: Flow,
    candidates: &mut Vec<Candidate>,
) -> Option<Flow> {
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Bind { names, value, .. } => {
                flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, value, &mut flow, candidates);
                for name in names {
                    flow.inputs.remove(name);
                    flow.checked.remove(name);
                }
            }
            Stmt::Expr { value, .. } => {
                flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, value, &mut flow, candidates)
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => {
                let (admitted, _) =
                    flow_condition(catalog, schema, arguments, condition, flow, candidates);
                flow = admitted;
            }
            Stmt::Return { value, .. } => {
                flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, value, &mut flow, candidates);
                return None;
            }
            Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, payload, &mut flow, candidates);
                if let Some(once) = once {
                    flow_expression(catalog, schema, arguments, once, &mut flow, candidates);
                }
            }
            Stmt::If {
                condition,
                then_body,
                else_body,
                ..
            } => {
                let (then_entry, else_entry) =
                    flow_condition(catalog, schema, arguments, condition, flow, candidates);
                let then_flow = flow_statements(
                    catalog,
                    schema,
                    arguments,
                    then_body,
                    then_entry,
                    candidates,
                );
                let else_flow = flow_statements(
                    catalog,
                    schema,
                    arguments,
                    else_body,
                    else_entry,
                    candidates,
        Stmt::For { name, domain, body, .. } => {
            helper_for(catalog, schema, name, domain, body, state, target, stack)
        }
        Stmt::As { principal, body, .. } => {
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, principal, &mut state, target, stack);
            helper_statements(catalog, schema, body, state, target, stack)
        }
        Stmt::Break { .. } => HelperFlow { broken: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() },
        Stmt::Continue { .. } => HelperFlow { continued: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() },
    }
}

fn helper_for(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, name: &str, domain: &Expr, body: &[Stmt], mut state: HelperState, target: &str, stack: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> HelperFlow {
    helper_expression(catalog, schema, domain, &mut state, target, stack);
    if matches!(domain, Expr::Array { items, .. } if items.is_empty()) {
        return HelperFlow { normal: Some(state), ..HelperFlow::default() };
    }
    let outer_alias = state.aliases.contains(name);
    let mut first = state.clone();
    first.aliases.remove(name);
    let mut header = first.clone();
    let mut returned = None;
    let mut broken = None;
    let mut iteration_exit = None;
    loop {
        let body_flow = helper_statements(catalog, schema, body, header.clone(), target, stack);
        returned = join_helper_states(returned, body_flow.returned);
        broken = join_helper_states(broken, body_flow.broken);
        let back = join_helper_states(body_flow.normal, body_flow.continued);
        iteration_exit = join_helper_states(iteration_exit, back.clone());
        let Some(mut back) = back else { break };
        // Every iteration introduces a fresh loop binding. Assigning that name
        // cannot restore or mutate the shadowed outer formal.
        back.aliases.remove(name);
        let next = join_helper_states(Some(first.clone()), Some(back)).unwrap();
        if next == header { break }
        header = next;
    }
    let restore = |state: Option<HelperState>| state.map(|mut state| {
        if outer_alias { state.aliases.insert(name.to_owned()); } else { state.aliases.remove(name); }
        state
    });
    let normal = join_helper_states(
        Some(state),
        join_helper_states(restore(iteration_exit), restore(broken)),
    );
    HelperFlow { normal, returned, ..HelperFlow::default() }
}

fn helper_condition(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, expression: &Expr, state: HelperState, target: &str, stack: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> (HelperState, HelperState) {
    match expression {
        Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => {
            let (yes, no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, value, state, target, stack);
            (no, yes)
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" => {
            let (left_yes, left_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, left, state, target, stack);
            let (right_yes, right_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, right, left_yes, target, stack);
            (right_yes, join_helper_states(Some(left_no), Some(right_no)).unwrap())
        }
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "or" => {
            let (left_yes, left_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, left, state, target, stack);
            let (right_yes, right_no) = helper_condition(catalog, schema, right, left_no, target, stack);
            (join_helper_states(Some(left_yes), Some(right_yes)).unwrap(), right_no)
        }
        Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. } if op=="=" || op=="!=" => {
            let mut evaluated=state;
            helper_expression(catalog,schema,expression,&mut evaluated,target,stack);
            let mut yes=evaluated.clone();let mut no=evaluated;
            let alias=match (left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()) {
                (Expr::Name {name,..},Expr::Literal {value:crate::ast::Literal::Null,..})|
                (Expr::Literal {value:crate::ast::Literal::Null,..},Expr::Name {name,..})=>Some(name),_=>None,
            };
            if alias.is_some_and(|name|yes.aliases.contains(name)) {if op=="=" {yes.checked=true;} else {no.checked=true;}}
            (yes,no)
        }
        _ => {
            let mut evaluated = state;
            helper_expression(catalog, schema, expression, &mut evaluated, target, stack);
            (evaluated.clone(), evaluated)
        }
    }
}

fn helper_expression(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, expression: &Expr, state: &mut HelperState, target: &str, stack: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
    if !state.checked && expression_must_lookup(catalog, schema, expression, &state.aliases, target, stack) {
        state.checked = true;
    }
}

fn join_helper_states(left: Option<HelperState>, right: Option<HelperState>) -> Option<HelperState> {

    /// edge multiplies by its cap, and edges into a table add up. `None` when
    /// the cascade graph from `parent` is cyclic or a cascade lacks its cap.
    pub fn closure_bounds(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Vec<(String, u64)>> {
        // Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.
        let mut reach: Vec<String> = vec![parent.to_owned()];
        let mut edges: Vec<(usize, usize, u64)> = Vec::new();
        let mut at = 0;
        while at < reach.len() {
            let table = reach[at].clone();
            for r in self.referencing(&table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                let cap = u64::from(self.reference_cap(r.child, r.column)?);
                let child = reach.iter().position(|t| t == r.child).unwrap_or_else(|| { reach.push(r.child.to_owned()); reach.len() - 1 });
                edges.push((at, child, cap));

            at += 1;
        }
        // Kahn's order over the reached cascade subgraph; a leftover table is a cycle.
        let mut indegree = vec![0_usize; reach.len()];
        for &(_, child, _) in &edges { indegree[child] += 1; }
        let mut bounds = vec![0_u64; reach.len()];
        bounds[0] = 1;
        let mut ready: Vec<usize> = (0..reach.len()).filter(|&t| indegree[t] == 0).collect();
        let mut done = 0;
        while let Some(table) = ready.pop() {
            done += 1;
            for &(from, child, cap) in edges.iter().filter(|(from, _, _)| *from == table) {
                bounds[child] = bounds[child].saturating_add(bounds[from].saturating_mul(cap));
                indegree[child] -= 1;
                if indegree[child] == 0 { ready.push(child); }
            }
        }
        (done == reach.len()).then(|| reach.into_iter().zip(bounds).collect())
    }

    pub(super) fn record_selection(&mut self, site: &str, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, result: &str, reason: &str) {
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let bindings = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(position, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(position) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        let origin = if self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())) { "synthesized; " } else { "" };
        let source = if origin.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; read: {}", self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| print::read_expr(read))) };
        let mut selection = format!("selected {} ({bindings}); result: {result}; {origin}{reason}{source}", index.name);
        if !self.variants.is_empty() {
            let when = self.variants.iter().map(|(name, present)| format!("{} {}", name.trim_start_matches("$intentPresent:").trim_start_matches("$intentSearch:"), if *present { "present" } else { "absent" })).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
            selection = format!("when {when}: {selection}");
        }
        self.selections.entry(site.into()).and_modify(|prior| { if !prior.contains(&selection) { prior.push_str("; "); prior.push_str(&selection); } }).or_insert(selection);
    }

    }
