//! Developer evidence is asynchronous and belongs to one live dev/store pair.
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use std::{fs, io::{BufRead,BufReader,Read,Write}, net::TcpStream, process::{Child,Command,Stdio,Output}, path::PathBuf, sync::{Arc,atomic::{AtomicUsize,Ordering}}, time::{Duration,Instant}};
const BIN: &str=env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_snapback4");
#[path = "dev_refusals/round3.rs"]
mod round3;
#[path = "dev_refusals/quiet.rs"]
mod quiet;
#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "dev_refusals/shutdown.rs"]
mod shutdown;
const SOURCE: &str="use personas alice\ntable rows:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nmutation add(owner: principal):\n  return insert rows {owner}\n";
static NEXT: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0);
struct Project(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Project { fn drop(&mut self) { let _=fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0); } }
impl Project {
 fn new() -> Arc<Self> {
  let dir=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-mp-refusal-{}-{}",std::process::id(),NEXT.fetch_add(1,Ordering::Relaxed)));
  fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("snapback")).unwrap();
  let mut source=SOURCE.to_owned();
  for n in 0..40 {source.push_str(&format!("mutation add{n}(owner: principal):\n  return insert rows {{owner}}\n"));}
  fs::write(dir.join("snapback/schema.q"),source).unwrap();Arc::new(Self(dir))
 }
 fn why(&self,args:&[&str])->Output {Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&self.0).args(["why","recent"]).args(args).output().unwrap()}
 fn record_path(&self,store:&str)->PathBuf {
  let legacy=self.0.join(".snapback4/dev-refusals.json");
  if legacy.exists() {return legacy;}
  fs::read_dir(self.0.join(".snapback4/dev-refusals").join(store)).unwrap().map(|e|e.unwrap().path()).find(|p|p.extension().and_then(|s|s.to_str())==Some("json")).unwrap()
 }
 fn wait_record(&self,store:&str,operation:&str)->Value {
  let deadline=Instant::now()+Duration::from_secs(10);
  loop {
   let path=self.record_path(store);
   let mut record:Value=serde_json::from_slice(&fs::read(path).unwrap()).unwrap();
   if record.is_array() {record=json!({"records":record,"instance":"legacy"});}
   if record["records"].as_array().unwrap().last().is_some_and(|r|r["operation"]==operation) {return record;}
   assert!(Instant::now()<deadline,"writer did not persist {operation}: {record}");
   std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10));
  }
 }
}
struct Host { child: Child, project: Arc<Project>, address: String, stderr: PathBuf }
impl Drop for Host {fn drop(&mut self) {let _=self.child.kill();let _=self.child.wait();}}
impl Host {
 fn start(project:Arc<Project>,dev:bool,blocked_stderr:bool)->Self {
  Self::start_with(project, dev, blocked_stderr, &[])
 }
 fn start_with(project:Arc<Project>,dev:bool,blocked_stderr:bool,extra:&[&str])->Self {
  Self::start_storage(project,dev,blocked_stderr,extra,true)
 }
 fn start_storage(project:Arc<Project>,dev:bool,blocked_stderr:bool,extra:&[&str],fresh:bool)->Self {
  if !dev {let out=Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&project.0).args(["deploy","release"]).output().unwrap();assert!(out.status.success(),"{}",String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr));}
  let stderr=project.0.join(format!("stderr-{}.log",NEXT.fetch_add(1,Ordering::Relaxed)));
  let sink=if blocked_stderr {Stdio::piped()} else {Stdio::from(fs::File::create(&stderr).unwrap())};
  let mut child=Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&project.0).args(if dev && fresh {vec!["dev","--fresh","--no-watch","--port","0"]} else if dev {vec!["dev","--no-watch","--port","0"]} else {vec!["serve","release","--port","0"]}).args(extra).stdout(Stdio::piped()).stderr(sink).spawn().unwrap();
  let mut line=String::new();BufReader::new(child.stdout.take().unwrap()).read_line(&mut line).unwrap();
  assert!(line.starts_with("http://"),"{line}: {:?}",fs::read_to_string(&stderr));
  Self{child,project,address:line.trim().trim_start_matches("http://").into(),stderr}
 }
 fn request(&self,operation:&str,owner:&str,auth:&str)->Value {
  let body=json!({"args":{"owner":owner}}).to_string();let mut s=TcpStream::connect(&self.address).unwrap();s.set_read_timeout(Some(Duration::from_secs(5))).unwrap();
  write!(s,"POST /m/{operation} HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\n{auth}\r\nContent-Length: {}\r\n\r\n{body}",body.len()).unwrap();
  let mut reply=String::new();s.read_to_string(&mut reply).unwrap();serde_json::from_str(reply.split_once("\r\n\r\n").unwrap().1).unwrap()
 }
 fn refuse(&self,operation:&str)->Value {
  let reply=self.request(operation,"dev:bob","x-snapback-persona: alice");assert_eq!(reply["why"]["code"],"E_RULE","{reply}");reply
 }
}
#[test]
fn dev_reports_source_locally_and_production_keeps_the_same_caller_refusal() {
 let dev=Host::start(Project::new(),true,false);
 let reply=dev.refuse("add");
 let store=reply["store_id"].as_str().unwrap();
 let envelope=dev.project.wait_record(store,"add");
 let records=&envelope["records"];
 assert_eq!(records[0]["clause"],"insert rule");
 assert!(records[0]["site"].as_str().unwrap().ends_with("schema.q:5:3"),"{records}");
 assert!(records[0]["executionSite"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with("add:"),"{records}");
 assert!(!records.to_string().contains("bob"));
 #[cfg(unix)] {use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;assert_eq!(fs::metadata(dev.project.record_path(store)).unwrap().permissions().mode()&0o777,0o600);}
 let why=dev.project.why(&[]);assert!(why.status.success(),"{}",String::from_utf8_lossy(&why.stderr));assert_eq!(serde_json::from_slice::<Value>(&why.stdout).unwrap(),envelope);
 let deadline=Instant::now()+Duration::from_secs(5);
 loop {let stderr=fs::read_to_string(&dev.stderr).unwrap();if stderr.lines().any(|l| l.starts_with("dev refusal: add E_RULE at ") && l.contains("schema.q:5:3 (insert rule on rows)") && l.ends_with(" — snapback4 why recent")) {break;}assert!(Instant::now()<deadline,"{stderr}");std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10));}
 assert!(!reply.to_string().contains("schema.q"));
 let prod=Host::start(Project::new(),false,false);
 let minted=Command::new(BIN).current_dir(&prod.project.0).args(["token","--service","mp-test","--release","release"]).output().unwrap();
 assert!(minted.status.success(),"{}",String::from_utf8_lossy(&minted.stderr));
 let token=String::from_utf8(minted.stdout).unwrap();
 let production=prod.request("add","bob",&format!("Authorization: Bearer {}",token.trim()));
 assert_eq!(reply["why"]["code"],production["why"]["code"]);
 assert_eq!(reply["why"]["family"],production["why"]["family"]);
 assert!(production["why"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with(reply["why"]["message"].as_str().unwrap()));
 assert!(production["why"]["message"].as_str().unwrap().ends_with("rows rule clause: insert <- .owner = viewer"));
 assert!(!prod.project.0.join(".snapback4/dev-refusals").exists());
 assert!(!prod.project.0.join("release/dev-refusals").exists());
 assert!(!fs::read_to_string(&prod.stderr).unwrap().contains("dev refusal"));
}
#[test]
fn simultaneous_fresh_instances_are_selected_and_stale_or_mismatched_records_rejected() {
 let project=Project::new();let mut a=Host::start(project.clone(),true,false);let b=Host::start(project.clone(),true,false);
 let refused=a.refuse("add");let store_a=refused["store_id"].as_str().unwrap();
 let record_a=project.wait_record(store_a,"add");let instance_a=record_a["instance"].as_str().unwrap();
 let success=b.request("add","dev:alice","x-snapback-persona: alice");let store_b=success["store_id"].as_str().unwrap();assert_ne!(store_a,store_b);
 let ambiguous=project.why(&[]);assert!(!ambiguous.status.success());assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&ambiguous.stderr).contains("Multiple live dev instances"));
 let own=project.why(&["--store",store_b]);assert!(own.status.success());let own:Value=serde_json::from_slice(&own.stdout).unwrap();assert_eq!(own["records"],json!([]));assert_ne!(own["instance"],instance_a);

I reproduced a key-resolution mismatch using the existing compiler binary: a required row expression becomes ambiguous when the table also has a unique reference to itself, while the equivalent explicit `.id` form compiles. I’m checking whether this is isolated and finishing the privacy and lint-proof review.

//! Writes: the four words, their rules, the constraints (unique, cap,
//! immutable, append-only, after-insert), maintained aggregates, and the
//! partition events every row change produces.

use super::runner::Runner;
use super::validate::validate_row;
use crate::error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError};
use crate::partition;
use crate::rows::index_values;
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir};
use crate::schema::{Constraint, Effect, Table, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::value::{Supplied, Value, canonical_json};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum Origin {
    Source,
    Maintained,
}

#[path = "write_references.rs"]
mod references;
#[path = "write_target.rs"]
pub(crate) mod target;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, false).map(|_| ())
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_if_absent(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, true)
    }

    fn insert_mode(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        if self.device && if_absent && self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("insert if absent on a read-through child queues without a row".into()));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            let mark = runner.meter.mark();
            let result = runner.insert_attempt(table_name, row, if_absent, None).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, Effect::Insert));
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — denied direct allocations hide deferred
            // read witnesses and counter work just as NOT_FOUND hides targets.
            if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Rule { effect: Effect::Insert, .. } | RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. }))
                && runner.schema.tables.get(table_name).is_some_and(|table| !table.sequences.is_empty()) {
                runner.meter.restore(mark);
            }
            result
        })
    }

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));
        // An independent denial must never consult a hidden read witness.
        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }

        let image_bytes = row.heap_bytes();

        if sequence_full && if_absent {

                index.unique && index.columns().iter().all(|column| !table.sequences.contains_key(column))

                    return Ok(false);
                }
        let requested = context.now.unwrap_or_else(crate::now_ms);

        if context.presence.is_none() {
            context.presence = self.presence.clone();
        }
        let tx = self.store.read_transaction()?;

            &self.schema,

        }

                let args = context.args.clone();

                let outcome = QueryOutcome { decoration_expiry: None, search_rows: None, search_scopes: None, delivery, delivery_meter, ephemeral: version, unavailable: runner.query_unavailable, complete: !runner.query_unavailable && runner.meter.capped_at.is_none() && runner.word_cut_at.is_none() && !runner.non_uniform && !runner.page_position_lost() && !runner.timeline_pending, cut: runner.read_cut(), capped_at: runner.meter.capped_at.clone(), word_cut_at: runner.word_cut_at.clone(), next: None, data, seq, metered: runner.meter.used, tables: runner.read_tables.iter().cloned().collect(), next_expiry: runner.next_expiry.get() };

            {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Native { code: "E_NATIVE".into(), message: format!("{name} is a native read and this server was built without the wasm host") }.into());
            }

    fn rebound_names(statements: &[Stmt], out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        for statement in statements {
            match statement {
                Stmt::Bind { names, .. } => out.extend(names.iter().cloned()),
                Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { rebound_names(then_body, out); rebound_names(else_body, out); }
                Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => rebound_names(body, out),
                _ => {}
            }
        }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
    }
    fn expression_guard_node(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, expr: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if !observe_prefix {
            for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) {
                input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, known, global, system, visiting, notes);
            }
        }
        if let Expr::Exists { read, site } = expr && (observe_prefix || point_probe(catalog, read)) {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: site.clone() }, known, system, notes);
        }
        if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, system, notes);
            expression_guards(catalog, schema, program, condition, known, global, system, true, visiting, notes);
        }
        for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
            let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
            if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { continue; }
            if let Some(inner) = helper_body(catalog, &name, shape) {
                // Parameters and focal rows belong to the definition's scope. Only
                // viewer/literal facts about the same world cross that boundary.
                let entry = if let Expr::Call { args, .. } = expr && !shape { call_facts(catalog, &name, args, known, global) } else { known.intersection(global).cloned().collect() };
                body(catalog, schema, program, &inner, entry, global, &guard_bindings(catalog, &inner), system, observe_prefix, visiting, notes);
            }
            visiting.remove(&key);
        }
    }

//! Engine-private write addressing, conditions and disclosure.
use crate::engine::runner::{Flow, Runner};
use crate::engine::rules::rule_probes;
use crate::error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError};
use crate::ir::{Builtin, Expr, IndexKeyExpr, Input, KeyProvenance, LoopDomain, Read, Scan, Stmt, WriteCondition};
use crate::schema::{ColumnType, Constraint, Effect, Table, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::rows::index_values;
use crate::value::Value;

// Only this resolver can certify caller-evaluated natural keys.
pub(crate) struct TargetInput(());

impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent and hidden-refused targets restore every logical meter field.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn target_attempt<T>(&mut self, table: &str, attempt: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        let capture_prepaid = std::mem::replace(&mut self.capture_prepaid, false);
        let prepaid = std::mem::replace(&mut self.maintain_prepaid, false);
        let previous_heap = std::mem::take(&mut self.maintain_heap);
        let column_prepaid = std::mem::replace(&mut self.column_prepaid, false);
        let column_heap = std::mem::take(&mut self.column_heap);
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        let assets_prepaid = std::mem::replace(&mut self.assets_prepaid, false);
        let result = self.reserve_columns(table).and_then(|_|attempt(self));
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        { self.assets_prepaid = assets_prepaid; }
        self.capture_prepaid = capture_prepaid;
        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(self.column_heap);
        self.column_prepaid = column_prepaid;
        self.column_heap = column_heap;
        self.meter.used.heap_bytes = self.meter.used.heap_bytes.saturating_sub(self.maintain_heap);
        self.maintain_heap = previous_heap;
        self.maintain_prepaid = prepaid;
        if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Require { code, .. }) if code == "NOT_FOUND") { self.meter.restore(mark); }
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.013#11.1 — reserve authorization before target existence or expiry can select a different outcome.
    pub(super) fn reserve_write_rules(&self, table: &Table, effects: &[Effect]) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let probes = effects.iter().map(|effect| rule_probes(table.rules.for_effect(*effect))).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
        if !self.meter.room(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::RuleProbes, actual: self.meter.used.rule_probes.saturating_add(probes), ceiling: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes, site: self.meter.site().into() });
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — reserve resolution's static worst case before the target lookup; no ceiling is changed.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn reserve_target_resolution(&mut self, table_name: &str, table: &Table, patch: &crate::value::Supplied, upsert: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if upsert { self.reserve_capture(table)?; }
        let probes = rule_probes(&table.rules.read).saturating_mul(2).saturating_add(rule_probes(&table.rules.update).saturating_mul(1 + u64::from(table.judges_old_image())))
            .saturating_add(if upsert || table.ephemeral.is_some() { rule_probes(&table.rules.insert).saturating_mul(2) } else { 0 });
        // Covers the largest cloned row allowed by the schema, the caller's patch
        // (including ill-typed fields), and the miss arm's image/default materialization.
        // Retained maps/assets share their Arc; the merge charges only memory it copies.
        let witness = self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read).max(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.update))
            .max(if upsert || table.ephemeral.is_some() { self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.insert) } else { 0 });
        let copy = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) { 0 } else { row_copy_ceiling(table).saturating_mul(2) };
        let heap = copy.saturating_add(patch.heap_bytes().saturating_mul(2))
            .max(crate::rows::disclosed::admission(table)).saturating_add(witness);
        for (kind, amount, used, ceiling) in [(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes, self.meter.used.rule_probes, self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes),
            (MeterKind::HeapBytes, heap, self.meter.used.heap_bytes, self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes),
            (MeterKind::IndexEntries, if upsert { table.sequences.len() as u64 } else { 0 }, self.meter.used.index_entries, self.meter.ceilings.index_entries)] {
            if !self.meter.room(kind, amount) { return Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind, actual: used.saturating_add(amount), ceiling, site: self.meter.site().into() }); }
        }
        self.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// The merged image and its lawful copy price (LLP 3000.015 §1), which a
    /// later engine copy of the same image charges again.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn merge_patch(&mut self, table: &str, old: &Value, patch: &crate::value::Supplied, _readable: bool) -> Result<(Value, u64), RuntimeError> {
        let public = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(&self.schema.tables[table]) {
            self.authorize_image(table, old.clone())?
        } else { None };
        let mut bytes = 40_u64;
        for (key, _) in old.as_object()?.iter().filter(|(key, _)| !patch.as_object().expect("patch checked").contains_key(*key)).chain(patch.as_object()?.iter()) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — a retained field's actual size is disclosed
            // only after both row and field read authority succeed.
            let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
            let copy = if patch.as_object()?.contains_key(key) { patch.field_copy(key) } else if let Some(row) = &public { row.scalar_heap(key) } else { definition.columns.get(key).map_or(0,column_copy_ceiling) };
            bytes = bytes.saturating_add(80 + key.len() as u64 + copy);
        }
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let fields = old.as_object()?.iter().filter(|(key, _)| !patch.as_object().expect("patch checked").contains_key(*key)).chain(patch.as_object()?.iter())
            .map(|(key, value)| (key.clone(), value.clone())).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>();
        Ok(source) => source,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read {file}: {error}"); return 2; }
    };
    let name = Path::new(file).file_name().and_then(|n| n.to_str()).unwrap_or(file).to_owned();
    match snapback4_lang::emit::lean::lean(&[(name, source)]) {
        Ok(module) => { println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&module.to_json()).unwrap()); 0 }
        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Diagnostics(problems)) => {
            for problem in problems { eprintln!("{problem}"); }
            1
        }
        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Unsupported(u)) => {
            eprintln!("outside the Lean model's fragment: {u}");
            1
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn problem_reports(bound: &Bound, explain_repeats: bool, verbose: bool) -> (Vec<String>, usize) {
    // Suppress redundant cascades first, so a hidden diagnostic never counts
    // towards the repeat threshold that earns an inline card excerpt.
    let mut hidden = 0;
    let mut shown_diagnostics = Vec::new();
    for (i, diagnostic) in bound.diagnostics.iter().enumerate() {
        if !verbose && diagnostic.rewrite == "fix this Quarry line and rerun snapback4 check"
            && diagnostic.cascade_of.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| bound.diagnostics[..i].iter().any(|earlier| earlier.code.starts_with("E_") && earlier.site == *cause)) {
            hidden += 1;
            continue;
        }
        shown_diagnostics.push(diagnostic.clone());
    }
    (diagnostic_reports(&shown_diagnostics, "", explain_repeats), hidden)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.1 — bring the diagnostic's own card to the builder at the repeated problem.
fn diagnostic_reports(diagnostics: &[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic], prefix: &str, explain_repeats: bool) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut counts = BTreeMap::new();
    for diagnostic in diagnostics {
        *counts.entry((&diagnostic.code, &diagnostic.guide)).or_insert(0) += 1;
    }
    // Two is the first recurrence; pre-counting puts help under its first site.
    // At most three existing bounded why excerpts add <= 12,291 bytes, including blank lines.
    let mut remaining = if explain_repeats { 3 } else { 0 };
    let mut bound_explained = false;
    diagnostics.iter().map(|diagnostic| {
        let mut report = format!("{prefix}{diagnostic}\n");
        if counts.remove(&(&diagnostic.code, &diagnostic.guide)).is_some_and(|count| count >= 2)
            && remaining > 0 && !(diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND" && bound_explained) && let Some(explanation) = diagnostic.explanation_section() {
            if diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND" { bound_explained = true; }
            report.push_str(&format!("\n{explanation}\n"));
            remaining -= 1;
        }
        report
    }).collect()
}

fn note_reports(notes: &[snapback4_lang::Diagnostic], verbose: bool) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut explained = std::collections::BTreeSet::new();
    notes.iter().map(|note| {
        let mut compact = note.clone();
        if !verbose && let Some((summary, _)) = compact.message.split_once('\n') {
            compact.message = format!("{}.", summary.trim_end_matches(':'));
        }
        let mut report = format!("note: {compact}\n  why: snapback4 why {}\n", note.code);
        if verbose && explained.insert((&note.code, &note.guide))
            && let Some(explanation) = note.explanation() {
            report.push_str(&format!("\n{explanation}\n"));
        }
        report
    }).collect()
}

fn print_prediction(bound: &Bound, verbose: bool) {
    let summary = if verbose { snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_summary(bound) }
        else { snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_overview(bound) };
    // Compiler temporaries (`sessions[$reference3]`) never print (LLP 3000.036 §15.6).
    if let Some(summary) = summary { println!("{}", crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, &snapback4_lang::bind::spell_internals(&summary), verbose)); }
}

fn note_summary(note: &snapback4_lang::Diagnostic) -> String {
    let Some((first, rest)) = note.message.split_once('\n') else { return note.message.clone(); };
    if note.code == "N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED" {
        let names = rest.lines().filter_map(|line| line.trim().strip_prefix("maintain ")?.split_once(':').map(|(name, _)| name)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let consequences = rest.lines().filter_map(|line| {
            let (_, consequence) = line.rsplit_once("; ")?;
            consequence.contains("would stop predicting").then(|| consequence.split(" (site ").next().unwrap())
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        return format!("{first} {}. Inherited sync has no horizon; online only loses offline coverage{}", names.join(", "),
            if consequences.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; {}", consequences.join("; ")) });
    }
    format!("{}.", first.trim_end_matches(':'))
}

fn completeness_note(note: &snapback4_lang::Diagnostic) -> Option<[String; 3]> {
    if !matches!(note.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE") { return None; }
    let verdict = if note.code == "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE" {
        "verdict: complete for the pinned audience; capped for other callers"
    } else {
        "verdict: capped; the server cannot prove that this page contains every readable row"
    };
    let admitted = ["for callers `", "except for callers `"].into_iter().find_map(|needle| {
        let rest = note.message.split_once(needle)?.1;
        rest.split_once("` admits").map(|(rule, _)| rule)
    });
    let who = match admitted {
        Some(rule) => format!("who: callers admitted by `{rule}` have a complete page"),
        None if note.code == "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE" => "who: callers admitted by the pinned parent guard have a complete page".into(),
        None => "who: no caller group is proved complete by this page".into(),
    };
    let what = if note.rewrite.starts_with("add `cap ") {
        format!("what: {}", note.rewrite)
    } else {
        "what: pin every read-rule scope or handle `complete: false` as more rows may exist; check --verbose shows the proof and checked repair".into()
    };
    Some([
        verdict.into(),
        who,
        what,
    ])
}

use crate::generate::Case;
use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Refusal, Store, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize, PartialEq)]
pub struct Outcome {
    pub kind: String,
    pub value: Json,
}
impl Outcome {
    pub fn meter(&self, field: &str) -> Option<u64> {
        self.value.get("metered")?.get(field)?.as_u64()
    }
    pub fn refusal(&self) -> Option<&Json> {
        self.value.get("refusal").or_else(|| self.value.get("why"))
    }
    pub fn next(&self) -> Option<&str> {
        self.value.get("next")?.as_str()
    }
    pub fn data(&self) -> Option<&Json> {
        self.value.get("data").or_else(|| self.value.get("result"))
    }
    pub fn cut(&self) -> bool {
        self.refusal().is_some_and(|r| r["code"] == "E_BOUND")
            || self.value.get("capped_at").is_some_and(|x| !x.is_null())
    }
}
pub fn compile(source: &str) -> Result<snapback4_lang::Bound, Json> {
    let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
    if bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
        Ok(bound)
    } else {
        Err(serde_json::to_value(&bound.diagnostics).unwrap())
    }
}
/// The clock the rows are seeded at.
pub fn seed_clock(case: &Case) -> i64 {
    case.seeded_at.unwrap_or(case.clock)
}
/// The seeded world before its prelude, at the case's budget.
pub fn seeded(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    let b = compile(source).map_err(|e| format!("binder: {e}"))?;
    let rows = case
        .rows
        .iter()
        .map(|(name, r)| {
            let table = b
                .schema
                .tables
                .get(name)
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("no table {name}"))?;
            wire::row_from_json(table, r)
                .map(|r| (name.clone(), r))
                .map_err(|e| e.to_string())
        })
        .collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
    let mut engine = Engine::new_at(
        Store::memory().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?,
        b.schema,
        b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program),
        seed_clock(case),
    )
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    engine
        .seed_at(rows, seed_clock(case))
        .map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    engine.set_ceilings(case.budget);
    Ok(engine)
}
/// The seeded world after its prelude, and the prelude's outcomes.
pub fn world(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<(Engine, Vec<Outcome>), String> {
    let mut engine = seeded(case, source)?;
    let outcomes = prelude(&mut engine, case);
    Ok((engine, outcomes))
}
pub fn engine(case: &Case, source: &str) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    world(case, source).map(|w| w.0)
}
/// Run the prelude in order on `engine`, each step at its clock and at the
/// default ceilings, sweeping first where the step says so, then sweep at
/// `clock` if the case says so. Returns one outcome per step and sweep (a
/// sweep's is the number of rows it removed). Leaves the case's budget set.
pub fn prelude(engine: &mut impl Executor, case: &Case) -> Vec<Outcome> {
    let mut out = vec![];
    for (i, step) in case.steps.iter().enumerate() {
        if step.sweep {
            out.push(sweep(engine, step.at));
        }
        engine.set_ceilings(snapback4_core::Ceilings::default());
        let viewer = step.viewer.as_deref().unwrap_or(&case.principal);
        out.push(execute_as(engine, case, &format!("prep{i}"), &step.args, viewer, step.at));
    }
    if case.sweep {
        out.push(sweep(engine, case.clock));
    }
    engine.set_ceilings(case.budget);
    out

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Optional(_)) }

    pub fn is_int(&self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Int | Self::IntRange { .. }) }

    pub fn unbounded(&self) -> ColumnType {

    pub fn asset_leaf(&self) -> Option<(crate::assets::AssetKind, u32, &[String])> {
        use crate::assets::AssetKind;
        match self {
            Self::Image { max, .. } => Some((AssetKind::Image, *max, &[])),
            Self::Video { max } => Some((AssetKind::Video, *max, &[])),
            Self::File { max, types } => Some((AssetKind::File, *max, types)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }

    pub fn contains_asset(&self) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Image { .. } | Self::Video { .. } | Self::File { .. } => true,
            Self::Optional(inner) | Self::List { of: inner, .. } | Self::ListRange { of: inner, .. } => inner.contains_asset(),
            _ => false,
        }

//! The Lean model's case format (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): a checked Quarry
//! program, translated from its **surface** syntax tree into the terms of
//! `snapback4/lean/Quarry/Syntax.lean`, as the JSON that
//! `snapback4/lean/Quarry/CaseJson.lean` decodes (format:
//! `snapback4/lean/README.md`, "The case format").
//!
//! **Why the surface tree.** The model is a second implementation of the
//! binder and the interpreter together. Reading the binder's IR would copy
//! the binder's decisions into the model's input, and the comparison could
//! no longer find a binder defect. So nothing here reads `bind` or the IR:
//! the input is the parsed [`Document`]s, and the only binder fact used is
//! that [`crate::compile`] reported no diagnostics, i.e. that the program
//! type-checks ([`lean`] refuses otherwise).
//!
//! **Types.** The few places that need a type (a text literal beside an id is
//! an id, OPEN V1; a positional key chooses a unique key by its argument
//! types, indexes card) take it from the surface's own declarations, which
//! the type checker has accepted: column types, argument types, helper
//! parameters, `viewer : principal`, and literal kinds, propagated through
//! locals by the small inference in [`Scope::ty`]. When that inference cannot
//! decide, the program is unsupported, never guessed.
//!
//! **The expansions** are those `Syntax.lean` documents: `public` is
//! `read <- allow`; an omitted rule is `deny`; an unnamed `update <- R` is
//! `R[old] and R[next]` (OPEN R2); positional keys become named equalities
//! on `id` (an `id`-typed or `t`-typed single key) or on the one declared
//! unique key whose column types accept the arguments in order; in a
//! `where`, each `.col = e` conjunct (e not reading the row) is a pin and the
//! remaining conjuncts are the filter; `return insert …` is a bound write
//! followed by `return`.
//!
//! **Erased, with the reason.** `sync …` and `online only` (residency
//! decides what a device holds, never a server outcome) and non-unique
//! `by` indexes (tuning; reads choose indexes themselves). A `text <= N`
//! bound is not modelled as a value restriction (OPEN W6):
//! [`LeanModule::store`] and [`LeanModule::tag_args`] refuse inputs that exceed
//! it, and a literal written past it is unsupported. The surface emitter does
//! use the bound to keep PAGE cases with nonportable synthesized keys out of
//! the fragment before the oracle. A computed text written to a bounded column
//! is not checked.
//!
//! **Everything else** outside the model's core fragment is an
//! [`Unsupported`] with a stable `reason` (counted by the harness, never a
//! pass) and a `detail` naming the construct. A translation is all or
//! nothing: there is no partial program.
// @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — the emitter half of the Lean oracle; surface tree in, model terms out

use crate::ast::{self, Document, Effect, Expr, KeyPart, Literal, ObjectField, OnDelete, Order, ReadExpr, Stmt, TypeKind, WriteOp};
use serde_json::{Map, Value as Json, json};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

/// A construct outside the model's core fragment.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct Unsupported {
    /// Stable, low-cardinality name (the harness counts by it).
    pub reason: String,
    /// The construct, for a human.
    pub detail: String,
}

impl Unsupported {
    fn new(reason: impl Into<String>, detail: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        Self { reason: reason.into(), detail: detail.into() }
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for Unsupported {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        write!(f, "{}: {}", self.reason, self.detail)
    }
}

pub fn check(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    if options.flags.contains_key("store") && options.flags.contains_key("release") {
        return releases::check_release(options);
    }

    if let Some(file) = options.flags.get("emit-lean") {
        return emit_lean(file);
    }
    let project = project_or_exit();
    let started = Instant::now();
    package_versions::warn(&project);

use super::*;

// @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — bounded live, authorized point reads are distinct
// from the immutable audience capture of read through.
pub(super) fn rule_reference(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, base: &Expr, field: &str, bindings: &RuleBindings, site: &Site) -> Fallible<(RuleTerm, Ty)> {
    let refusal = |reason: &str| Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP", reason,
        reference_rewrite(catalog, info, &Expr::Member { base: Box::new(base.clone()), name: field.into(), site: site.clone() })).guide("rules");
    let (source, ty) = match base {
        Expr::Member { .. } => rule_term(catalog, info, base, bindings)?,
        _ => return Err(refusal("a rule hop needs a row-local reference or an id lookup through it")),
    };
    let (column, old, mut hops) = match source {
        RuleTerm::Field(c) | RuleTerm::NextField(c) => (c, false, vec![]),
        RuleTerm::OldField(c) => (c, true, vec![]),
        RuleTerm::Reference { table, column, field, old } => (column, old, vec![RuleHop { table, field }]),
        RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, hops, old } => (column, old, hops),
        _ => return Err(refusal("a rule hop needs a row-local reference")),
    };
    if hops.len() >= MAX_RULE_HOPS { return Err(refusal("a rule term follows at most three references")); }
    let Ty::Ref(table) = ty.required() else { return Err(type_error(site, "a rule hop needs a table reference", "declare the source column with its referenced table type")); };
    let target = catalog.tables.get(&table).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("unknown table {table}"), "declare the referenced table"))?;
    let (owner, source_field) = hops.last().map_or((info, column.as_str()), |hop| (&catalog.tables[&hop.table], hop.field.as_str()));
    let private = |info: &TableInfo, field: &str| info.decl.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == field && c.written_by.is_some() && !c.readable_by_audience);
    if !bindings.hops { return Err(refusal("read rules inherit whole parent authority with read through; live hops belong to write rules")); }
    if target.maintained.is_some() || super::super::columns::fields(target).any(|c| c == field) {
        return Err(refusal("a rule hop cannot read maintained targets or fields at a fixed point-read cost; pass the view's group keys to the mutation"));
    }
    if target.decl.ephemeral.is_some() { return Err(refusal("ephemeral rows cannot decide durable write authority; store this authority in a durable table before using the mutation check")); }
    if private(owner, source_field) || private(target, field) { return Err(refusal("a rule hop cannot disclose a private field to the row audience")); }
    let field_ty = column_ty(target, field).ok_or_else(|| unknown(site, format!("{table} has no column {field}"), name_rewrite(field, target.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)))?;
    if hops.is_empty() { return Ok((RuleTerm::Reference { table, column, field: field.into(), old }, field_ty)); }
    hops.push(RuleHop { table, field: field.into() });
    Ok((RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, hops, old }, field_ty))
}

pub(super) fn reference_rewrite(catalog: &Catalog, info: &TableInfo, expr: &Expr) -> String {
    fn lookup(catalog: &Catalog, table: &str, key: &str) -> String {
        // Aggregate rows are addressed by their group, never by a row id.
        let group = catalog.tables.get(table).filter(|t| t.maintained.is_some())
            .and_then(|t| t.indexes.iter().find(|i| i.name == "byGroup"));

    }
    fn private_guard(info: &TableInfo, field: &str, row: &str, lines: &mut Vec<String>) {

                    named = read.keys.first().and_then(|key| key.column.clone()).filter(|column| column != "id");

    pub fn refusal(&self) -> Refusal {

                refusal.rule = Some(code.clone());

                refusal.detail = Some(detail.clone());
                refusal.guide = Some("indexes".into());
            }
            Self::Require { code, site, message } => {
            _ => self.clone(),
        }
    }

impl RuntimeError {
    pub(crate) fn authorization_context(self, table: &str, effect: Effect) -> Self {
        match self {
            Self::AuthorizationBudget { .. } => Self::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect },
            other => other,
        }
    }

    /// Serde's error channel is text. Preserve the schema codec's stable
    /// capability refusal when a host adds input or durable-store context.
    pub fn decode(error: serde_json::Error, fallback: fn(String) -> Self) -> Self {

     4	impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
     5	    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
     6	        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
     7	        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
     8	            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
     9	            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
    10	            let (e, t) = match expected {
    11	                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
    12	                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
    13	            };
    14	            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
    15	            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
    16	            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
    17	        }

    22	                }
    23	            }
    24	        }
    25	        Ok(())
    26	    }

### 5.2 Stage 2: a small Lean core

PAGE2 extends this operation fragment under [LLP 3000.036.001 §15](./3000.036.001-merged-pages-for-owner-or-x-lists.rfc.md#15-page2-ordinary-exact-memberships-row-ids-and-parent-owners) and [LLP 3000.036.003 §7](./3000.036.003-time-audiences-and-timed-disclosure.rfc.md#7-formal-and-generated-semantics): exact membership/byId classification and pinned-clock lower read lifetime, including physical write occupancy. Durable opening publication and clock-floor protocol remain runtime/simulator/device obligations, not Lean proof claims.

**Scope.** It starts after Stage 1's generator covers the core fragment.
The core is the value model, expressions, point reads and scans in key
order, the four rules, and the completeness flag. It is a Lake project at
`snapback4/lean/` with a pinned toolchain and no Mathlib unless a proof
needs it.

**2026-10-13 — FLAKE oracle refresh.** Every default-path `lean::refresh`
holds one blocking file lock under this checkout's ignored `.lake` directory
until `lake build quarry-oracle` exits. The lock covers both threads and
processes and is retained, never unlinked after release. Concurrent cold
refreshes previously rewrote the same generated C/object files: the default-
thread test suite saw null-filled C input, missing objects and compiler crashes.
Each refresh still checks current sources; there is no once-per-process cache
that could conceal a model edit. Explicit `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE` retains its
caller-vouched bypass. A stale/corrupt local build is repaired before validating
the fix; no model or engine semantics change.

**The proofs:**

- determinism;
- the value order is total and agrees with the key encoding;
- no read discloses a row its rules hide;
- no write succeeds without write authority;
- `complete: true` is never reported for a read that omitted a qualifying
  row.

W13g then **proved the boundary cannot be inferred from row images** (the stream serves current images, so a mutable ordering key can move below the floor before a device sees it; two histories with different floors produce byte-identical payloads) and the wave 13 lead authorised an additive snapshot field carrying each completed horizoned group's floor (lane W13g-2, the field under the same two-family review). **Landed on main 2026-09-17 from the reviewed tip `e4f274b479` after five blind rounds (§20.22.7); the acquisition's selection time is put to the author there.** | LLP 3000.007.001 §3.6 stated its rule before the measurement: past 10%, a device-side repair is designed and reviewed first. **NOT READY** at `f32eea9800`.

- **Child-owner proof misses parent deletion and recreation.** [owner_equality.rs:6](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs:6) treats immutable ownership as stable across the parent’s lifetime. With `on delete keep`, Alice can create a parent and child, delete the parent, and Bob can recreate its id with himself as owner. Bob’s readable parent now has an unreadable child, but [line 49](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/owner_equality.rs:49) suppresses `N_UNREADABLE_PROBE`. Identity protection covers only read-through children ([write.rs:175](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/write.rs:175), [through.rs:114](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/through.rs:114)). Retain the note unless the proof also establishes that parent deletion/recreation cannot break ownership equality.

- **Implicit row keys can disagree with explicit `.id`.** [positional_keys.rs:7](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/positional_keys.rs:7) gives identity precedence to `Id`/`Ref`, while admitting rows as candidates for other reference keys. Reproduced: with `parents.sibling: parents ?` and `unique sibling`, `parents[parents[p] ! else NOT_FOUND]` refuses with two candidate keys; `parents[(parents[p] ! else NOT_FOUND).id]` compiles. The existing shortcut handles only names ([program.rs:1128](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program.rs:1128)). Give required same-table row expressions intrinsic-id precedence while preserving single evaluation.

- **Nested alternatives invalidate the refusal-order proof.** [write_affordances.rs:17](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs:17) unions owner equalities across both `and` and `or`; [line 22](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs:22) intersects only top-level disjuncts. Consequently, `(.owner = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops') and viewer != 'blocked'` incorrectly counts ownership as mandatory and emits the note despite the seat legitimately reaching `NOT_OWNER`. Compute mandatory equalities recursively: union for conjunction, intersection for alternatives.

- **Service-seat clause formatting bypasses ephemeral mutations.** [engine/mod.rs:865](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/mod.rs:865) returns `error.refusal()` directly for ephemeral mutations, bypassing the static-clause append at [line 977](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w11-qdiag/snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/mod.rs:977). A service denied an ephemeral table insert therefore receives E_RULE without the promised clause. Apply the same cause-independent formatter to this refusal path.
